# Awesome Prediction Market Tools: item-by-item integration audit

**Item-by-item browser audit:** 2026-10-25 UTC  ·  **Inventory revalidated:** 2026-10-26 UTC  ·  **Entries:** 179 unique projects (178 categorized listings plus the featured Oddpool entry).
**Upstream snapshot:** `aarora4/Awesome-Prediction-Market-Tools` at `e900f5d576c9341b144b9f3cade5f0871be79471` (read-only clone).

**Active cockpit target:** Continue cockpit implementation on the unversioned `/` and `/index.html` handlers in `scripts/cockpit.py`, which serve `scripts/cockpit_ui.html`. Older audit notes may mention existing versioned diagnostic pages; they are not the active cockpit surface for follow-up work.

## Method and limits

Every listed URL was opened once using the project-mandated headless Playwright CLI. For each, this report records the README description, HTTP/navigation result, current page title where available, and an individual integration decision. A `200` only proves that a page responded; it does not prove the advertised feature works, data is complete, or performance claims are true. A `403`/`429`/connection failure means the page could not be inspected in this browser pass—not that the project is necessarily dead. Non-code commercial pages were assessed from their accessible landing/docs surface; they were not treated as audited services.

The page-status sample was: 134 HTTP 200, 19 HTTP 403, 5 HTTP 404, 1 HTTP 429, and 20 unresolved DNS/SSL/navigation results. These totals describe the original browser pass only; they are not a verdict on quality or whether a blocked service is still operating. ## What is worth integrating

1. **TREMOR’s movement-radar concept, adapted locally.** Its source describes a real-time price/volume monitor with multiple horizons, but its published hand-set score thresholds are not a calibrated predictive metric. PolySparQ++ already records CLOB snapshots and public prints. The root Predictions tab and V5 offer a **Window tape** panel with both UP and DOWN midpoint histories in 15-second buckets, movement from the first stored quote, captured prints/notional, and book freshness. The root cockpit additionally switches to stored spread, top-of-book size imbalance, or locally inferred public-trade flow. Flow signs combine UP buys with DOWN sells as UP-supporting, and the inverse as DOWN-supporting; unknown aggressors are counted but omitted. The root also has a compact **Live Window Pulse** for current BTC/ETH/SOL/XRP 5m or 15m markets, with selectable 30/60/90-second movement comparison, exact baseline age, quote freshness, and a configurable browser-local visual threshold. The root Markets Watchlist now offers the same 30/60/90-second choice over fresh CLOB midpoint traces; its signed move compares the latest sample to the first sample in the chosen range, and visible sample counts expose partial history. It also shows per-outcome public-print counts and gross share×price over that selected receive-time lookback, using only validated events observed while the browser stream was connected; missing browser coverage is labeled partial, and the count is never presented as total market volume. The pulse can optionally order cards by the largest fresh observed move within each horizon and expand an UP-book readout for current and comparison bid, ask, spread, and top-level share sizes. These displays stay descriptive, not calibrated forecast scores or order gates. Rolling 5/15-minute markets use locally relevant horizons rather than TREMOR’s 1h/24h ranges.
2. **Oracle3 / Eventarb’s explicit per-leg fee analysis, adapted locally.** Oracle3 calculates fees and net edge for named relationships; Eventarb similarly exposes per-leg fees and payout. The root and V5 Prediction Boards show a read-only one-share UP + DOWN baseline. The root now also walks the visible ask ladders for 1, 5, 10, and 25 complete-set shares, calculates fees across consumed price levels, and reports total and per-pair costs/edge plus visible paired depth. The root card also shows current UP and DOWN best bid, best ask, and spread, plus a compact top-level share-depth comparison, from the same book snapshot. Its Window Tape adds a paired-midpoint parity trace: UP midpoint + DOWN midpoint − $1, matched only at identical recorded 15-second bucket indices. It is a descriptive midpoint comparison, not executable edge. Unsupported fee schedules and stale/missing books remain unavailable; quote depths are cleared when book age is unknown or exceeds three seconds. The UI rounds each side's fee estimate upward, does not credit taker rebates, and labels the read as visible-book research rather than a fill promise. The current root card links to Polymarket's published fee rules and identifies the round-up as a conservative local estimate; the official policy specifies five-decimal precision and a $0.00001 minimum but not a rounding mode. These estimates do not submit orders and do not model book changes or one-leg-only fill risk. No third-party code was copied. Polymarket’s published fee formula, market-level schedule fields, and binary payout are the source for the independent implementation.
3. **Marketlens / Probalytics historical depth as an offline backfill candidate, not a live dependency.** Rechecked their current first-party docs on 2026-10-25. Marketlens documents point-in-time L2 reconstruction and historical event replay; its free key is limited to markets open within seven days and two million data rows once, while full-archive access is described as Pro. Probalytics advertises full-depth book states on each change and a 1ms replay model; its Orderbook plan is listed at $249/month and its three-day trial only includes the most recent seven days. These capabilities could improve retrospective replay because our own recorder is optional, 1Hz-limited, and stores at most 20 levels. A source audit also found the separate collector discarded full depth and treated changed levels as complete captures; the collector repair below now reconstructs each token book from its full seed plus subsequent deltas. The root Markets tab and V5 Feed Quality now report bounded local coverage for up to 500 tracked 5m/15m windows closed in the last 24h: any snapshots, windows with both outcomes, and occupancy of 15s buckets with both outcomes since each market's first-seen time. A root-card disclosure drills into the 12 most recent windows for each asset and horizon, puts the weakest coverage first, summarizes incomplete and untracked windows, shows per-window snapshot and two-sided bucket counts, and links to Polymarket when the stored slug exists. Each detail includes a 15-second timeline from first tracked sample to window close, marking both outcomes, UP-only, DOWN-only, or no snapshot; it reports the longest consecutive span without both books and its accessible label counts each bucket state. The root panel can bypass the short cache on manual refresh. This surfaces long gaps and one-sided book capture while explicitly avoiding a continuous-cadence or full-depth claim. The root Window Tape now also replays those locally captured 15-second buckets in chronological order for visual review and downloads the currently displayed window as a versioned JSON research bundle containing its raw locally stored market-motion fields, server snapshot time, 15-second sampling declaration, and explicit inference/coverage caveats; this export is bounded by recorder coverage, not vendor historical depth, and contains no account or credential data. Do not add either vendor to live feeds or execution; verify a representative offline sample and its timestamps/provenance before considering paid access. No API key, account, paid query, SDK, or vendor dependency was added.
4. **Borrow mobile interaction patterns, not hosted services.** MobyScreener, Polycool, and Polymtrade present phone-first screening or trading experiences, but their checked pages did not expose data contracts suitable for this engine. The root cockpit adapts the useful presentation idea with a bottom navigation dock, phone-sized prediction cards, a default-on **Comfortable Reading** mode on first mobile use with a device-local compact override, a direct WINDOW LOG / SCORECARD switch, and per-window mobile probability traces comparing recorded model UP and CLOB UP checkpoints. The initial single MODEL STATUS card expands to the full phone content width when there is no forecast metrics response; a populated multi-metric strip remains horizontally swipeable. On mobile, five asset filters stay in one row, the four 5m/15m current/next window filters use a 2×2 grid with 44px targets, and the alarm has its own row. Live root forecast cards show timestamp-derived elapsed/remaining progress; the existing 55%-remaining alarm is marked at 45% elapsed on current 5m/15m cards and follows the alarm toggle. Upcoming cards remain distinct from live elapsed time. Each root Predictions market card also has a one-tap **FOCUS** action that isolates that asset, horizon, and window, saves the exact selection locally, marks the selected market, and reveals a one-tap **SHOW ALL MARKETS** reset. A focused view can be bookmarked or shared as a whitelisted `prediction_focus` URL built from a fresh unversioned-root URL, so the link carries only the selected asset, horizon, and period; opening it returns directly to the root Predictions page, and browser Back/Forward restores the prior filter state. On mobile, sharing uses the native share sheet or clipboard fallback, and SHARE FOCUS / SHOW ALL controls are at least 44px high. The Predictions phone view now keeps the global bot filter available in a shorter row, shortens the terminal ticker, and keeps cash-flow and live-fill strips visible, giving forecast controls and the first card more of the initial viewport. Root Markets also has an on-demand searchable catalog using the existing Gamma proxy; it labels Gamma outcome prices as last trades and links to the venue without adding an order action. Its device-local watchlist saves up to 12 validated condition IDs, refreshes each exact market through the existing authenticated read endpoint, and labels returned prices with the catalog-check time and cache state. A user-toggleable 30-second refresh keeps those catalog snapshots current only while the root Markets → Watchlist view is foregrounded; users can switch it off. The watchlist stores no quotes or private data and adds no execution action. A share action creates a clean link to the unversioned root containing only validated public condition IDs; opening it imports into the device-local list and immediately shows Markets → Watchlist, while removing the share parameter from the address bar. Import preserves existing saved IDs and visibly reports items omitted at the 12-market cap. The catalog also sorts loaded results or refreshed saved snapshots by 24-hour volume, liquidity, or earliest end; missing metrics and unavailable end dates sort last, and the UI states that sorting applies only to loaded rows. Each root forecast card can also expand up to three newest existing RSS headlines whose title directly mentions that asset; this borrows market-linked news presentation without consuming an external feed, and labels matches as context rather than a relevance score. The root Bot Fleet presents session net P&L prominently, enlarges phone selectors and action controls, and reflows wallet and supervisor details without changing their handlers. These are local display and navigation changes; no external app feed or execution component is used.
5. **Avoid external “win rate”, smart-money, AI edge, copy-trade, and arbitrage claims as order inputs.** The listing pages did not establish comparable, timestamped, after-fee, independently reconciled results. No third-party execution bot, trading SDK, custody service, leverage protocol, or social signal was connected. For operator diagnostics, the unversioned root Overview now shows the latest locally recorded Oracle, Coinbase, and Binance tick per asset, its age, and the one-hour sample count; it reports missing history and clock-ahead timestamps separately. This is local recorder freshness, not proof of an upstream socket connection or forecast quality, and it does not enter a strategy or order gate.
6. **Official account-level cross-check for operational context.** Wallet/portfolio concepts recur across entries such as PredScan and Zapper, but their listed pages did not provide a verified integration contract. Polymarket publishes an unauthenticated [Data API v2 contract](https://data-api.polymarket.com/v2/docs) with cursor-paginated wallet positions and a distinct portfolio-value endpoint. The root Positions page adds an optional admin-only, manual lookup against those first-party read routes. It can help inspect exchange-side holdings beside the local bot ledger, but it is not an accounting reconciliation: a wallet can contain manual, transferred, combo, and non-PolySparQ positions, and `/v2/value` includes combo positions at cost basis rather than free cash. Its position cards now disclose the fee-exclusive entry basis, attributed buy fees, all-in cost, and realized P&L on demand. The labels follow the API contract (`total_pnl = realized_pnl + unrealized_pnl`; `unrealized_pnl = current_value - entry_cost_usdc`); fees are shown for disclosure and must not be deducted twice. Marked value is explicitly distinguished from executable sale proceeds. No wallet address is prefilled, persisted, or sent until the operator explicitly loads it; no credentials or write endpoint are used.
7. **Compare official last-trade side with local tape inference.** Polymarket's current real-time market schema includes an `OrderSide` on `last_trade_price` events and illustrates `BUY`/`SELL`, but does not define that field as a guaranteed taker-aggressor classification. The root Window Tape now retains that optional venue-reported value separately from the existing BBO tick-rule inference, reports side coverage and notional, and shows their agreement rate when both classifications exist. This is a recorder-quality cross-check and a transparent view of two different data fields; it does not silently relabel the local inference, change predictor/PAPER-fill inputs, or create an order signal. No third-party code or database migration is needed; the side is stored in the existing JSONB raw payload. See [Polymarket Real-Time Data](https://docs.polymarket.com/market-data/realtime-data).
8. **Keep optional event-time provenance visible.** Polymarket documents the last-trade timestamp as nullable. The market recorder now keeps valid exchange time, falls back to callback receive time when it is missing, and stores both values plus the selected timestamp source in the existing raw JSONB. The root Window Tape counts exchange timestamps, receive-time fallbacks, database fallbacks, and older rows with no provenance tag, so a fallback cannot be mistaken for venue event time. The main engine's rolling trade-flow buffer also uses the resolved timestamp; PAPER maker fill simulation already had the same receive-time fallback and retains it. This prevents missing event timestamps from becoming 1970 records or falling outside the active-window tape while preserving the distinction between event time and observation time. No schema migration or order-path change is needed.
9. **Keep optional print-size coverage visible.** The same documented event makes trade size optional. The feed parser now distinguishes a parsed finite, nonnegative size from an absent or malformed one; storage retains unavailable sizes as zero only to fit the existing non-null column and tags them in raw JSONB. The root tape reports parsed, missing, and legacy-unknown size counts alongside notional, so unknown sizes cannot be mistaken for real zero-size prints or hide a notional-coverage gap. No fabricated estimate or execution behavior is introduced, and no database migration is needed.
10. **Make the root cockpit installable without making private data available offline.** The existing phone-first cockpit can now be added from the browser to an Android home screen as a standalone app. Its manifest and branded icons use only the root `/` scope and are served behind the cockpit's existing authentication gate. No service worker, cache, credentials, bot data, or offline trading screen was added; the installed cockpit remains network-dependent, so it cannot present stale account state as current.
11. **Measure forecasts over time and at the decision checkpoint.** Analytics in the list commonly compare forecasts against resolved outcomes; Octagon AI's listing also foregrounds model probability beside live market price, but its values were not shown to be an auditable feed. The root Predictions history reports resolved-only direction hit rate among forecasts whose stored predicted-side probability meets an adjustable 50–95% threshold, alongside qualifying sample count and coverage, for each checkpoint. Each live/current-next card also offers a bounded page-session chart of model UP probability beside a separate fresh CLOB midpoint, with missing-data gaps visible. A compact `Δ pp` chip now gives the latest Model UP movement against a fresh page-session sample when the observations are no more than 12 seconds apart; it disappears when the forecast is stale or the comparison gap is longer. These views support inspection of the user's 60% checkpoint and live forecast changes without treating them as profitability evidence or changing a bot gate.
12. **Borrow Simmer's reproducibility label, not its trading service.** The [Simmer SDK README](https://github.com/SpartanLabsXyz/simmer-sdk) documents a `config_hash`, baseline comparisons, and explicit replay-realism gaps. Its own README says its backtests use trade tape without order books and that its tape coverage ends around 2026-05-05. PolySparQ++ already has a root Strategies editor and configuration audit trail, so the useful low-risk concept is a copyable SHA-256 fingerprint of redacted profile settings. The root editor now shows that fingerprint, deliberately excludes credentials, and says it does not cover engine defaults or runtime overrides. A follow-up adds a server-side comparison with a fresh PID-matched bot heartbeat containing the last-loaded source digest; the browser sees only “in sync” or “file ahead,” never either digest. Matching source bytes still does not prove every setting is effective, because some values require restart or are outside the engine's hot-reload subset. TOML secret fields in nested maps and array tables are redacted as well; raw editor text hides a whole credential-bearing table. No Simmer key, package, tape, SDK, trading route, or copied code was added; these labels identify profile inputs and loaded source, not a runtime strategy result or profitable backtest.
13. **Polymarket Analytics / Falcon API: documented, but defer integration.** The listed analytics homepage returned HTTP 403, but its separate [Falcon API quickstart](https://api.polymarketanalytics.com/quickstart) documents authenticated semantic-retrieval endpoints for wallet metrics, trades, candles, historical order books, market insights, and social signals. The quickstart says a free API key is available, specifies `https://retriever.falconapi.net/api/v2/semantic/retrieve/parameterized`, and documents 401/429 handling as correcting credentials or reducing request rate with backoff. The linked [API context file](https://prediction.heisenberg.so/prediction_market_api_context.md) specifies the same endpoint path at `https://narrative.agent.heisenberg.so`; an unauthenticated, non-personal `agent_id=574` request to each host returned the same HTTP 401 response shape. This confirms both hosts expose an auth-gated route, but does not prove equivalent service, data, account scope, or that one host supersedes the other. Neither docs surface establishes request quotas, freshness/latency guarantees, retention, or commercial terms. The context file defines PnL as realized-only, excluding open positions. Keep the provider out of cockpit data and bot gates until the vendor clarifies host/limits/terms and the operator intentionally provisions a key; no account, key, authenticated data request, package, or vendor dependency was added. The cockpit already provides local, provenance-labeled market tape and official Polymarket read routes for its operational views.
14. **PolyInsider's notable-trade presentation, adapted from captured local events.** The current [methodology](https://polyinsider.io/en/methodology) defines a whale alert as a $5,000 single buy later checked against that wallet's on-chain position, and a fresh-wallet alert as a separate $500/one-market pattern. It also says large bets are not proof of information, notes its P&L figures may be before fees, and warns that public data can lag. Its published whale [RSS feed](https://api.polyinsider.io/feeds/whales.xml) opened on 2026-10-26 but had no current items. The local root Predictions tape now exposes the 40 largest per-window prints whose sizes were explicitly captured, with a selectable notional floor and separately labeled venue-reported side and BBO-inferred side. The root Markets Watchlist now also has a device-local minimum gross-print control, filtering its short browser-observed CLOB sample by a selectable $100/$500/$1K/$5K floor. Operators can sort saved markets by their largest qualifying print within the same selected lookback and floor; markets without a captured qualifying print stay last. Our stored trade rows have no wallet identity and may represent individual prints rather than complete positions, so the UI deliberately says “public print,” never “whale” or “insider.” This is human research context only; no external feed, wallet inference, predictor input, or order gate was added.
15. **Markium's resolution-rule inspection pattern, implemented from first-party venue metadata.** Markium advertises an AI Rules Analyzer, but its current chat requires sign-in and no public analyzer API was shown. The root Market Explorer now expands the market's bounded Gamma `description` and `resolutionSource` verbatim, with a safe HTTP/HTTPS source link when applicable and a reminder to review the full official listing. This uses the existing public Gamma response, makes no AI-generated interpretation, and submits no order. Polymarket's [research data guide](https://institute.polymarket.com/data) identifies Gamma as the source for market resolution criteria and resolution source; Gamma may leave the source blank, which the cockpit shows explicitly.

16. **Compose's circuit-breaker visibility, adapted to our local runtime.** Compose advertises circuit breakers and self-healing order workflows, but its service would add unnecessary money-moving infrastructure. The root Fleet displays the PID-matched heartbeat's actual PAPER/LIVE/REPLAY mode, autopilot state, session-loss halt, and unresolved-order halt, with an explicit unknown/stale state when the heartbeat is missing or old. The root Overview now also surfaces each in-scope bot's local persistence-queue depth/capacity and per-source rejected-write counters, so the operator can see when recorded telemetry is dropping or the writer is backing up. Missing/stale counters remain unknown, and “scanner on” is not permission to enter: existing market, feed, strategy, sizing, price, and risk gates still apply. The mobile More dock keeps the latest root Radar alert count and severity visible across tabs, with an explicit unknown/stale marker. No third-party workflow engine, execution behavior, or trading gate was added.

17. **Pykalshi's bounded retry pattern, adapted to public Gamma reads.** The source implements retries for 429/5xx responses and capped WebSocket reconnect backoff. The root Market Explorer's Gamma cache already falls back to a last-known value and marks it stale, but used to issue another upstream request after every cache expiry even while rate-limited. The cache now applies a shared cooldown across Market Explorer Gamma cache keys after 429 responses, honors `Retry-After` in either seconds or HTTP-date form (capped at 300 seconds), and uses per-key bounded exponential delay for transient server/network failures; successful reads clear per-key state. During cooldown it serves an available cached result as stale, or fails fast if no cached value exists. This is limited to public market discovery/detail reads and does not touch forecasts, trading gates, or orders. Kalshi-specific book mapping was not reused.
18. **Betmoar's grouped event browser, adapted locally.** The public app groups many outcomes under one event, making context easier to scan. Root Markets now groups local Gamma results by event slug/title, labels each group with its visible child-market count, and links to the official event page. Child cards retain status, last-trade labeling, saved-market action, and exact resolution text. No undocumented Betmoar feed or trading control is reused.
19. **Synthesis's event detail layout, adapted to first-party CLOB data.** Its public event screen presents price, chart, order book, trades, outcomes, and resolution context together. Root Markets now offers a one-tap, manual top-of-book lookup per binary child market, with separate best bid/ask/spread values and a one-second freshness label that changes to stale at 15 seconds. The expanded detail also has a collapsed, read-only live print tape fed by the same single-market public CLOB stream, with outcome, venue-reported side, price, shares, gross value, separate event/receive times, and a validated transaction link. It retains at most 20 in-memory events, shows an advancing age for the newest browser-received print, and leaves the accessible print rows untouched during book-only updates; BUY/SELL is not assumed to identify the taker/aggressor. This reuses the existing official Polymarket book and market-channel feeds; no Synthesis endpoint, copytrading feature, wallet, or order action is used.
20. **Large-trade context, implemented from Polymarket's public Data API v2.** A separately surfaced PolyMaster GitHub project describes large-trade monitoring, but its connection to the listed product is unverified and the repository has no declared license, so no source was reused. The root Market Explorer now lets an operator open an on-demand **Recent Public Trades** panel for the exact market condition ID. It requests at most 20 rows per call from the documented first-party [Data API v2 trades endpoint](https://data-api.polymarket.com/v2/docs), validates the market identifier and returned condition when present, bounds numeric and text fields, sorts returned rows newest-first, and provides public wallet and Polygonscan transaction links when their identifiers validate. Its manual **Load Older Trades** action follows the documented signed keyset cursor while resending the exact same condition filter; the UI stops if the API repeats a cursor. Rows show outcome, BUY/SELL side, price, shares, gross notional, and event time. The panel adds a small gross-notional split for BUY versus SELL across currently loaded API rows, with row counts and an explicit warning that API-reported side is not an inferred aggressor signal. A short bounded in-memory cache limits duplicate upstream reads. The panel explicitly distinguishes indexed public trades from this bot's fills, a complete wallet position, and an executable quote. It is manually opened and paged, not polled or durably stored, and is not used as a trading signal or connected to order logic. No API key, wallet credential, third-party code, or new vendor was added.
## Current API compatibility check

On 2026-10-26, Polymarket’s official documentation banner confirmed Data API v1 had been retired on October 24, 2026. The root cockpit uses documented Data API v2 routes for optional account context and now for the on-demand public trade panel; it does not use a v1 positions route or legacy closed-positions/activity route. Gamma and CLOB remain the market catalog and quote sources. No migration was needed. See the [official migration guide](https://docs.polymarket.com/migrate/data-api-v1-to-v2).

## Follow-up candidate review

**Polyguana resolved-markets archive: do not add as a live feed or independent resolution verifier.** On 2026-10-26, the first-party [dataset landing page](https://polyguana.net/resolved/data), [published schema](https://polyguana.net/data/resolved-markets-schema), and [Terms of Service](https://polyguana.net/legal/terms) were rechecked. The schema identifies `market_id` as a Polymarket market identifier and `resolution_label` / `resolution_price` as the final outcome; `resolution_source` is a stored source or oracle reference where available, and every sampled row labels its export source `polyguana-resolved-archive`. Those fields can help cite a historical archive row, but do not establish an independent second settlement authority or add a signal before a market resolves. The schema does not publish a separate data license. The terms restrict automated scraping to the published datasets/interfaces and their terms, and restrict unagreed commercial use. No integration, recurring download, strategy input, or accounting override is justified by this evidence. Revisit only if a specific archival validation need arises and reuse rights are confirmed.

**Adanos first-party contract recheck (2026-10-26 11:02 UTC):** inspected the live docs and OpenAPI UI in Playwright at v1.54.1. The introduction lists `/reddit/crypto/v1` but only `/polymarket/stocks/v1`; the Reddit Crypto section documents daily activity, a current-3-day versus prior-3-day trend, and a 90-day baseline for `buzz_score`. The free-key limits remain 250 calls/month, 100/minute, and 30 days of history. These data are useful for slower research context, but no 5m/15m crypto-market coverage or predictive calibration is documented; the API-key quota and no current data gap do not justify adding a vendor panel or runtime dependency. No API credential was used and no sentiment-data endpoint was called.

**Root Market Explorer public-trades panel (2026-10-26 12:46 UTC):** adapted market-specific trade context as an independent read-only feature using the official public Data API v2 route. The new `/api/mobile/trades` endpoint accepts only a validated condition ID and a page size capped at 20, filters mismatched returned condition IDs, validates price/size/time and public address/hash fields, computes gross notional from shares × price, sorts the returned page newest-first, and uses a 12-second in-memory cache capped at 64 keys. The root Market Explorer exposes it through a manual per-card disclosure, with event time, side/outcome, price, shares, gross notional, and validated Polygonscan links; its copy says these indexed records are not bot fills, full wallet positions, or executable quotes. Added five backend/route/filter/cache/error tests to the focused Market Explorer suite. Validation passed: 12 focused tests, cockpit Python compilation, inline JavaScript syntax (`node --check`), `git diff --check` for the edited paths, and the online audit validator (179 upstream items matched 179 ordered entries with all required fields). A Playwright CLI preview of the local static root HTML used a seeded synthetic market and mocked trade response: at 390px the document/card/panel measured 390/298/268px and refresh/hide targets were 46px; at 320px they measured 320/228/198px with no horizontal overflow. Screenshot review showed the rows and disclosure legible at 320px. Static preview API 404s elsewhere were expected; there was no authenticated runtime, live trade result, production deployment, bot, or order verification. The preview browser and server were closed.

**Root Market Explorer public-trades cursor pagination (2026-10-26 12:51 UTC):** inspected the official [Data API v2 docs](https://data-api.polymarket.com/v2/docs) in Playwright. The current `/v2/trades` contract is cursor-only: `pagination.next_cursor` is a signed opaque keyset cursor, `has_more` is exact, the cursor carries its page size and seek anchor, and callers must resend the identical filters for each page. A live public `GET /v2/trades?limit=1` returned the documented envelope and an opaque next cursor. Added a manual **LOAD OLDER TRADES** action to each root Market Explorer public-trades disclosure. Each page remains capped at 20; the server validates bounded cursor text, keeps the same condition filter, and caches each page briefly. The browser appends and time-sorts rows, offers older pages only when `has_more` and a valid cursor are present, and stops on a repeated cursor. Two new backend tests cover cursor rejection and cursor round-trip; all 14 focused Market Explorer tests pass, as do Python compilation, inline JavaScript syntax, and scoped `git diff --check`. A Playwright CLI fixture exercised one initial and one older page, verified newest-first order and the end-of-history state, and measured no horizontal overflow at 390px or 320px; the load-older control measured 46px at both widths. The preview was local static HTML with mocked trades, so its unrelated API 404s were expected; no authenticated runtime, private wallet, order, or deployment was involved. Browser and preview server were closed.

**Root Market Explorer public-trades rate-limit handling (2026-10-26 12:54 UTC):** adapted the bounded retry/backoff pattern already used for Gamma discovery to public Data API v2 trade pages. The backend honors `Retry-After` for HTTP 429 (seconds or HTTP-date, capped at five minutes), applies per-page exponential cooldown to retryable 5xx/network failures, and serves an available expired page as explicitly stale. When no cached page exists it returns a retry delay; the Market Explorer now tells the operator that the feed is rate-limited and when to retry. This is a manual read-only panel and has no forecast, bot, or order dependency. All 16 focused tests passed; Python compilation, inline JavaScript syntax, and scoped `git diff --check` passed. Playwright CLI static-root fixtures displayed the 429 message, a cached stale trade, and a 502 retry message at 390px with no horizontal overflow. The default Python environment lacks `psycopg2`, so the focused suite used import-only in-memory stubs. The static server produced expected API 404s; no authenticated service, production rate limit, bot, or order was exercised.

**Root Market Explorer paired CLOB snapshot (2026-10-26 13:04 UTC):** the official [CLOB OpenAPI spec](https://docs.polymarket.com/api-spec/clob-openapi.yaml) documents batch `POST /books` with token IDs in a JSON array; its [rate-limit page](https://docs.polymarket.com/api-reference/rate-limits) distinguishes `/books` from single `/book`. A live read-only check against a current Gamma market found that a two-token GET query returned `400 Invalid payload`, while documented POST returned `200` with both `asset_id` rows and a shared timestamp. Added the root-only `/api/mobile/books` proxy: it validates the binary market and token IDs, fetches both outcomes in one POST, maps the response back into the market's original outcome order, validates returned token/condition identifiers, and uses a one-second cache plus bounded 429/5xx cooldown and stale fallback. The Market Explorer now renders both sides from one browser request; the existing single-outcome `/api/mobile/book` route used by manual-order freshness checks remains unchanged. All 23 focused tests pass. A live call through the new backend function returned two fresh outcome books; a Playwright static-root fixture confirmed one batched browser request, correct Yes/No order, and no horizontal overflow at 390px. This does not verify authenticated production routing or place an order.

At 12:52 UTC, a fresh Playwright visit to the exact user-designated `https://polymarketaipredictions.com/` redirected to the public marketing page at `www.polymarketaipredictions.com`. Its Fleet cockpit link points to `https://lightning.polymarketaipredictions.com/`. This anonymous view does not verify authenticated routing; implementation remains in the local unversioned `/` handler and `scripts/cockpit_ui.html`, never `/v3`–`/v5`.

## Implementation and verification record

| Research concept | PolySparQ++ change | Boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Root CB Edge Live readability | Coinbase and oracle readings are now separate labeled quote tiles with source-update ages, delta, the existing per-asset threshold, threshold proximity, median oracle lag, and available paired-sample count. On phones, this panel follows Active Markets and precedes the candle chart. Signal badges preserve the existing absolute-delta threshold check; unsupported asset keys are ignored and missing readings stay unavailable (scripts/cockpit_ui.html). | Reuses the existing read-only /api/cb_edge response and browser thresholds. No predictor, bot, order, or backend behavior changed. |
| Root mobile Engine Lab workspace | Performance summary cards use a legible two-column phone grid; accuracy, live signal, prediction, and configuration sections have a thumb-sized jump rail. Diagnostic rows stack labels above values, and engine-instance, refresh, configuration, and save controls are enlarged for touch (scripts/cockpit_ui.html). | Mobile presentation and local section navigation only. Forecast calculations, predictor values, config-write handlers, and engine state are unchanged. |
| TREMOR movement radar | Root Predictions Window Tape: in-window UP/DOWN midpoints, selectable spread, size imbalance, and BBO-inferred public-trade net notional with per-bucket classification coverage; plus per-side movement, captured print counts/notional, and quote age. V5 retains the midpoint tape (`scripts/cockpit.py`, `scripts/cockpit_ui.html`, `scripts/cockpit_v5.html`). | Descriptive; inferred aggressor is not an exchange flag, unclassified prints are excluded, and no flow value enters a forecast or order gate. |
| Root Live Window Pulse sorting | The root Predictions radar can order existing pulse cards by the largest absolute fresh UP-midpoint change within each 5m/15m group; unmeasured cards follow in stable asset order. Asset order remains the default, and the selection is browser-local (`scripts/cockpit_ui.html`). | Sorting only rearranges captured descriptive samples. It does not infer direction quality, predict outcomes, add a feed, or affect orders. |
| Polymarket reported trade-side comparison | The root tape parser preserves the optional official last-trade `BUY`/`SELL` side as separate telemetry in `pm_trades.raw.reported_side`; the engine and collector resolve the token to its UP/DOWN market side before persisting. The read-only root `/api/market_motion` aggregate reports venue-side flow and compares it with local BBO tick-rule inference. The root Predictions tape shows reported-side coverage, signed notional, and side agreement beside inferred flow (`src/feeds/polymarket_md.*`, `src/main.cpp`, `src/collector/main.cpp`, `src/db/pg.*`, `scripts/cockpit.py`, `scripts/cockpit_ui.html`). | No schema migration: uses existing raw JSONB. Local BBO inference remains the existing predictor/PAPER-fill source. The venue field is an `OrderSide`, not treated as a guaranteed taker flag; all added measures are descriptive and cannot gate or submit orders. |
| Optional trade timestamp provenance | Main-engine flow calculations and collector persistence now prefer a positive exchange timestamp and use callback receipt time only when it is missing. The DB insert records `exchange_ts_ms`, `received_ts_ms`, and `timestamp_source`; the root API aggregates source counts and the Predictions tape displays exchange, receipt-time, database-clock, and untagged-history coverage (`src/feeds/polymarket_md.hpp`, `src/main.cpp`, `src/collector/main.cpp`, `src/db/pg.*`, `scripts/cockpit.py`, `scripts/cockpit_ui.html`). | Existing raw JSONB only; no schema migration. PAPER maker fills already preferred exchange time and used receive time as fallback, and retain that behavior. Legacy rows remain explicitly untagged. No LIVE path, predictor gate, or order decision changed. |
| Optional trade-size coverage | The feed parser now tracks whether the optional event size was present, finite, and nonnegative. Missing/malformed values remain zero in the non-null legacy column but are tagged `size_available=false` in raw JSONB; the read-only root tape reports known, missing, and legacy-unknown sizes alongside the all-print notional (`src/feeds/polymarket_md.*`, `src/main.cpp`, `src/collector/main.cpp`, `src/db/pg.*`, `scripts/cockpit.py`, `scripts/cockpit_ui.html`). | No schema migration; no notional is invented. Missing sizes are visible coverage gaps. No forecast, PAPER fill qualification, LIVE execution, or order decisions change. |
| Root cockpit home-screen install | Added a same-origin web app manifest, purpose-built 192px/512px Android icons and Apple touch icon, an authenticated root asset allowlist, and a browser-native install action in the mobile More drawer when Chromium emits `beforeinstallprompt`. Android/iOS browser-menu guidance remains available when the event is unsupported or declined. | No service worker or private-data caching; root URL only; authentication and online data access remain required. The native event path was exercised with a synthetic browser event, not an actual device install. |
| Confidence-gated forecast evaluation | Root prediction-history scorecards report captured/resolved directional hit rate at a selectable 50–95% predicted-side confidence floor, with qualifying count, coverage denominator, and nominal 95% Wilson interval for each of six checkpoints. The default 60% cutoff and 60%-elapsed checkpoint align with examining the 5m two-minutes-remaining gate (`scripts/cockpit.py`, `scripts/cockpit_ui.html`). | Read-only use of stored checkpoint probabilities and resolved outcomes. The interval assumes independent Bernoulli observations and does not adjust for serial dependence; small samples remain uncertain. Forecast hit rate is not trade return and does not change strategy, bot, or order behavior. |
| Oracle3 / Eventarb fee and payout analysis | Root and V5 Prediction Board one-pair fee baseline; root walks visible UP/DOWN asks for 1/5/10/25 complete-set shares and reports per-level costs, fees, visible paired depth, and net edge. The root adds a separate UP/DOWN directional size walk at those sizes: independent model probability, visible ask cost and fees, model-implied expected payout, expected net value, and break-even probability. It also shows UP/DOWN best bid, ask, and spread, top quote share depth, and paired UP+DOWN midpoint deviation from $1 only in matching 15-second buckets (`scripts/prediction_board.py`, `scripts/cockpit_ui.html`, `scripts/cockpit_v5.html`). | Requires supported fee metadata and a fresh book. Depth scenarios describe only captured visible asks and do not promise fills. Directional EV assumes calibrated model probabilities and binary $1/$0 settlement; the model has not established an executable edge. Midpoint parity excludes asks, fees, and execution costs. No BBO/depth field signals execution or removes one-sided risk. |
| Marketlens / Probalytics historical depth | Root Markets and V5 Feed Quality coverage for up to 500 recent tracked 5m/15m windows. Root distinguishes any-snapshot from both-outcome buckets, counts fully covered windows, and provides a gaps-first disclosure that counts incomplete and untracked recent windows, with an accessible first-tracked-to-close 15-second UP/DOWN/no-snapshot timeline, a per-window longest span without both books, Polymarket links, and local chronological playback of stored tape buckets (`scripts/cockpit.py`, `scripts/cockpit_ui.html`, `scripts/cockpit_v5.html`). The separate collector also reconstructs complete depth from a full seed plus deltas and persists configured levels at the existing 1 Hz limit. The root Predictions pulse disclosure adds a proportional best-level bid/ask size bar, displayed-size imbalance, and size-weighted microprice from captured UP BBO/top-size fields; when current and baseline books are fresh and complete it reports change in imbalance percentage points and microprice cents. The root Markets Explorer now shows the same best-level visible depth for both outcomes from one paired CLOB snapshot (`scripts/cockpit_ui.html`). | Bounded, cached, local, read-only coverage and playback of captured buckets; manual root refresh bypasses the cache. Pulse and Market Explorer metrics are single-level descriptions, not full-depth queue modeling. Missing size remains unavailable and true zero remains zero; no zero-denominator imbalance is invented. Change deltas are suppressed for stale or incomplete snapshots. Playback does not fill gaps, recover unrecorded depth, or claim vendor-grade historical replay. A separate Marketlens sample-data README review found 17.3M claimed rows, with its BTC sample alone described as 12.64M rows / 111.2 MB for one day; intermediate book changes are folded between snapshots/trades, and its crypto reference tape is Binance rather than this project's Coinbase feed. The local environment has no Parquet reader installed. That sample is a possible bounded offline-research input after provenance/schema review, not a live dependency; no dataset, package, key, or vendor service was added. |
| Gamma-backed root Market Explorer | Root Markets now searches active public listings through the already-used `/api/mobile/markets` proxy, with paginated cards for question, outcome labels, Gamma last-trade prices, end date, volume, liquidity, and order-acceptance state; safe market slugs open the corresponding Polymarket event (`scripts/cockpit_ui.html`). | Operator-triggered read-only discovery, not an executable quote. Last-trade prices are explicitly distinguished from current bids/asks; stale-cache state is surfaced. It reuses the existing public Gamma proxy and adds no key, provider, polling loop, or order path. Polymarket's [data guide](https://institute.polymarket.com/data) describes Gamma as the market-discovery API and `slug` as the readable URL identifier. |
| Betmoar event-board browsing pattern | Root Market Explorer preserves each search result's Gamma event slug and groups matching child markets beneath a single event header with a visible market count and safe event link. Existing per-market save, status, last-trade values, volume/liquidity, and resolution details remain available (`scripts/cockpit.py`, `scripts/cockpit_ui.html`). | Search presentation only, using the current public Gamma-backed endpoint. Synthetic Playwright data rendered 2 event groups and 3 market cards at 1280px, 390px, and 320px; the 320px page stayed 320px wide, event links stayed 44px tall, and child cards stayed inside their group. Watchlist remains market-by-market; sorting still orders markets before grouping. No Betmoar endpoint, account, API, category feed, order route, or bot behavior was added. |
| Synthesis live-market detail pattern | Root Market Explorer cards fetch both binary outcomes through one on-demand `/api/mobile/books` batch and render the returned books in market outcome order. The panel shows each outcome's best bid, best ask, spread, quote age, best-level bid/ask share counts, a proportional depth bar, top-level imbalance, and size-weighted microprice; an expandable ladder shows up to eight price levels per side with cumulative shares and quote value, updating from the selected market's stream; it labels snapshots stale after 15 seconds and supports manual refresh. The same selected-market stream also feeds a collapsed live public-print tape, capped at 20 in-memory events with separately labeled event and receive times, venue side, outcome, price, shares, gross value, a Polygonscan link only for a valid transaction hash, and advancing age for the latest received print; book-only updates do not rebuild the accessible print log (`scripts/cockpit.py`, `scripts/cockpit_ui.html`). Missing sizes show `—`, measured zero sizes show `0 sh`, and unavailable zero-denominator metrics remain `—`. | Uses the official CLOB `POST /books` read route, Gamma market-detail lookup, and public market WebSocket `last_trade_price` messages documented [here](https://docs.polymarket.com/api-reference/wss/market); no Synthesis endpoint. The separate single-outcome `/api/mobile/book` path used by manual-order validation is unchanged. Gamma prices remain labeled as last trades. Book depth and public trade-side labels are descriptive, not a fill guarantee, forecast, inferred aggressor direction, or order gate. The tape follows one selected detail and discards events that fail market-token and numeric validation. No bot, order, account, credential, or LIVE setting is connected. |
| Root Markets multi-market live Watchlist stream | The unversioned root Market Explorer now uses one public CLOB WebSocket to stream paired quotes for up to 12 saved binary markets (24 outcome tokens) whenever Markets → Watchlist is foregrounded. It routes book snapshots and level deltas to each collapsed card, advances per-outcome quote ages, shows a state-colored accessible paired-book live count, and dynamically adds or removes token subscriptions without reconnecting. The expanded detail reuses that socket; only its selected market feeds the bounded public-print tape. The stream pauses when the page, Markets tab, or Watchlist is inactive (`scripts/cockpit_ui.html`). | Bounded and read-only; only validated two-outcome markets and decimal token IDs enter the subscription. It does not persist quotes or feed forecasts, bots, strategies, or orders. Polymarket's [public Market Channel docs](https://docs.polymarket.com/api-reference/wss/market) document multi-asset requests, subscription updates, book snapshots, price deltas, trade messages, and the 10-second heartbeat. Mocked Playwright verified the 24-token cap, no 13th-market subscription, two-token unsubscribe/subscribe deltas, collapsed quote updates, selected-detail tape, and pause after leaving Watchlist. At 320px the local root preview had no horizontal document overflow. Synthetic frames and a static backend do not verify a live exchange connection, authenticated page, or deployment parity. |

Initial verification on 2026-10-25; follow-up checks on 2026-10-26:

- Revalidated the upstream `main` SHA on 2026-10-26 and compared current README name/URL pairs with the report: all 178 categorized entries match in order and featured Oddpool is separately present; the report has 179 sequential entries, each with exactly one `Listed`, `Checked`, and `Assessment` field.
- Added a repeatable standard-library validator for report coverage. It passed against the current upstream README and report (179/179); a negative fixture with `Assessment` removed from the Oddpool row failed as expected.
- Reopened Polymarket's official migration guide in Playwright on 2026-10-26; its current banner confirms Data API v1 retirement on October 24, 2026. A repository source scan still finds no Data API host, v1 positions route, or legacy closed-positions/activity route.
- Inspected the `scripts/cockpit.py` root handler and opened the public apex in Playwright: local `/` serves `scripts/cockpit_ui.html`, while the anonymous public request redirected to the www marketing page. No authenticated production cockpit session was used in this check.
- Rechecked Polymarket’s current first-party fee documentation in Playwright. It lists a 0.07 Crypto taker-fee parameter and gives the symmetric price curve that peaks at 1.75 USDC for 100 shares at 0.50; the implementation’s per-share fee `rate × price × (1-price)` matches that example. The docs say charges are rounded to five decimals and that takers may separately receive tiered rebates, so the root copy identifies its rounded-up estimate as gross and excludes rebates.
- The Simmer source review documents `readonly()` and trading methods, but trading requires its external service/API key and live Polymarket execution also requires a wallet key; its tape-only backtests do not reproduce order-book queue/partial-fill behavior. No Simmer dependency or credential was introduced. Its configuration-fingerprint concept was implemented locally in the root Strategies editor, followed by private loaded-source telemetry and a read-only drift state; focused tests and a 320px browser preview are recorded below.
- The new root Strategies fingerprint regression tests cover canonical output, API-key/private-key redaction including array-of-table data, credential rotation, TOML dates, and settings changes. The UI preview confirms the 12-character label, full 64-character clipboard copy, scope disclosure, and no horizontal overflow at 320px. Three fingerprint tests plus the three root source-age and five history-scorecard tests passed (11 total); Python compilation, inline JavaScript syntax, and scoped `git diff --check` passed. Browser requests to unrelated API routes in the local static preview returned expected 404s; no authenticated cockpit or deployment is claimed.
- Passed 43 focused tests across market motion, market storage, capture status, pair economics, archive coverage (including the unversioned root route and refresh), cockpit review, and data accounting. Python compilation, root inline JavaScript syntax (`node --check`), and `git diff --check` also passed.
- **Prediction checkpoint scorecard follow-up (2026-10-25):** added the root history API's lookback options (1, 5, 30 days, or all history), a model-version filter, and a mobile-first scorecard for all six stored checkpoint times. Direction accuracy counts only captured UP/DOWN model calls with resolved outcomes; the paired model/CLOB comparison uses identical records and excludes exactly 50% CLOB ties; paired Brier scores require both probabilities. Older releases can be compared separately; pending, unavailable, missing, and mixed-version data are never silently treated as losses or as a single current-model score.
- Passed 47 focused cockpit/accounting tests, including four new scorecard tests. Python compilation, both root inline JavaScript blocks, and `git diff --check` passed. A disposable PostgreSQL 18 fixture verified the real aggregate SQL: a 5-day sample excluded the 6-day-old row, counted three model calls with two correct, omitted one 50/50 CLOB quote from directional comparison, and returned paired Brier values of 0.2333 (model) and 0.1900 (CLOB) over three paired forecasts. The temporary cluster was stopped and removed; production PostgreSQL was not queried or changed.
- Playwright rendered the root cockpit history modal with mocked scorecards. At 390×844 the scorecard uses a 335px swipeable card and 40px range controls; at 320×720 the document remains 320px wide with a 275px card rail. Switching to 1D sent `score_days=1` and updated the selected range label. This was a local static preview with mocked data; other API calls returned expected 404s from the static server, so it does not validate the authenticated service or production data.
- **Root complete-set depth scenarios (2026-10-25):** the root Predictions card now walks the captured UP and DOWN ask ladders for 1, 5, 10, and 25 paired shares; fees are charged per consumed price level and rounded upward once per outcome leg. Thin depth is shown as unavailable with each side's captured visible share count. The feature is research-only and does not touch order or bot logic. The focused suite now passes 48 tests; Python compilation and `git diff --check` pass. A synthetic Playwright preview rendered the root card at 390×844 with four scenarios in a two-column layout and no horizontal page overflow. It used mocked market data; the static preview's unrelated cockpit API calls returned expected 404s, so this is not a live-service check.
- **Root best-quote context (2026-10-25):** the root Predictions card now shows UP and DOWN best bid, best ask, and their spread using the card's existing book fields. Missing, malformed, or crossed quotes render unavailable. This is descriptive only; no API, bot gate, execution, or V5 route changed. Validation: the focused suite passes 48 tests; Python compilation, inline JavaScript parsing, and `git diff --check` pass. Mocked Playwright previews at 390×844 and 320×720 showed no horizontal overflow; at 320px the compact three-value quote and unit label fit without clipping. Crossed and missing quote cases rendered unavailable. The local static preview's unrelated API calls returned 404s, so no live service is claimed.
- **Top-of-book depth context (2026-10-25):** the root Prediction card now compares displayed bid/ask share sizes for UP and DOWN using relative bars. The API derives size only at each displayed best quote, ignores malformed/non-finite size rows, clears sizes and BBO prices with stale/unknown book data, and the UI independently suppresses quotes/depth once book age exceeds three seconds. This supports liquidity inspection without implying deeper liquidity or fill likelihood. The 49-test focused suite, Python compilation, inline JavaScript parsing, and `git diff --check` pass. Mocked Playwright previews at 390×844 and 320×720 stayed within viewport width; the 320px preview showed fresh quotes and depth while suppressing both for a synthetic 4.2-second-old book. Browser calls to unrelated APIs returned the expected 404s from static serving, so no authenticated/live service is claimed.
- **Model-version comparability follow-up (2026-10-25):** added a model-version selector to the scorecard. Its options come from captured, resolved checkpoints in the selected asset/horizon/lookback; legacy rows without a version are identified explicitly. Filtering applies to direction counts, paired accuracy, and paired Brier, while the full history table remains intact. The default “ALL MODEL VERSIONS” is named in the scope label so it cannot be mistaken for one current release.
- Verified the version-filtered SQL against another disposable PostgreSQL 18 fixture. Selecting `model-a` returned two 5-day samples and one correct direction; paired model/CLOB accuracy was 1/2 vs 2/2 and paired Brier was 0.225 vs 0.160. The same query offered `model-a` and `model-b`; a 6-day-old row was excluded. The temporary cluster was stopped and removed.
- Playwright with mocked history data showed the version selector populated with current and legacy options. Selecting `model-b` sent `score_model=model-b` and changed the scope label; at 390×844 the document stayed 390px wide, and at 320×720 it stayed 320px wide with a 294px selector, 40px range controls, and a 275px scorecard rail. Other page API calls returned expected 404s from the static preview; no authenticated or deployed behavior is claimed.
- Rechecked upstream `main` at `e900f5d576c9341b144b9f3cade5f0871be79471`; the report still contains 179 sequential project entries, each with `Listed`, `Checked`, and `Assessment` fields.
- Added a resolved-only forecast reliability chart to the root Predictions history. It bins one selected checkpoint's UP probabilities into ten ranges, plots model and same-window CLOB observed UP frequencies against the perfect-calibration diagonal, and exposes per-bin mean forecasts, outcomes, and sample counts. Both lines use exactly the same rows with a captured model and CLOB probability. Asset/horizon, model version, and date range scope the samples. The view is descriptive and does not feed the strategy or execution paths.
- Passed the 47 focused cockpit/accounting tests after adding calibration response, query-filter, route, and UI coverage. Python compilation, root inline JavaScript parsing, and `git diff --check` passed. The exact calibration aggregation SQL ran against disposable PostgreSQL 18 tables: model-version filtering excluded `model-b`, the expected model bins and matched CLOB bins had the expected counts and observed frequencies, and cleanup stopped and removed the temporary cluster. No production database was queried or changed.
- Playwright with mocked history data rendered the calibration chart, its model/CLOB markers and tooltip values, and the 60%-elapsed checkpoint selector. Selecting 40% sent `score_checkpoint=elapsed_40pct`. At 390×844 and 320×720, the document stayed at the viewport width; the calibration chart resized to the content column and the checkpoint control remained 40px tall on the narrower viewport. This was a local static preview with mocked history; unrelated API calls returned expected 404s and no authenticated or deployed behavior is claimed.
- Tightened the calibration query after review: rows without a CLOB probability are excluded from the model line as well as the CLOB line. A disposable PostgreSQL fixture with one missing CLOB checkpoint and a second model version confirmed that both series used exactly four matching windows; the incomplete row and other model version were excluded.
- Added a bounded CSV export for the visible prediction-history page, including every checkpoint's capture status, timing, probability, side, benchmark quote, model metadata, and details. Spreadsheet formula-leading text is prefixed safely; external strings are quoted and escaped. Playwright downloaded a mocked one-window CSV; Python's CSV parser verified 115 columns, all six checkpoint groups, and lossless round-tripping of a slug containing a comma and quotes. Node assertions also verified formula-leading text protection while preserving negative numeric fields. The generated fixture file was removed.
- A 320×720 Playwright pass exposed that the history modal's footer could fall below the phone viewport while body scrolling was locked. The modal now scrolls vertically on mobile and keeps its export/pagination footer sticky; the 320px document remained 320px wide and the three footer controls rendered at 40px high. The static mock preview returned expected 404s for unrelated cockpit APIs; no authenticated or deployed behavior is claimed.
- Improved the root complete-set fee panel using the already captured CLOB books: it now computes 1/5/10/25-pair scenarios by walking both visible ask ladders, including depth-based price impact and upward-rounded per-leg taker fees. Insufficient depth is shown explicitly. This stays read-only and does not change the V5 route, prediction, or execution gates.
- Ran the archive-coverage SQL against disposable PostgreSQL 18 fixtures. The aggregate query returned 24 two-sided buckets of 27 expected and one fully covered window of two; the updated nested response also returned the complete and gappy markets’ slugs, time bounds, snapshot rows, side counts, and bucket counts in newest-first order. Both temporary clusters were stopped and removed; no production database was queried or changed.
- Ran the updated bucket-map query against a disposable PostgreSQL 18 cluster. It returned 20 both-sided slots for the complete window and separate UP-only and DOWN-only slots for the gappy window; the temporary cluster was stopped and removed.
- Follow-up Playwright CLI review of the root Markets card with a mocked local-coverage response showed the new distinction directly (30/40 buckets with any snapshot, 24/40 with both outcomes, and 1/2 fully covered windows). This is a local static preview, not live cockpit data.
- Expanded the per-window disclosure in the mocked root-cockpit preview: a gappy window sorted ahead of a fully covered one, timestamps/counts and encoded Polymarket links rendered, and at 390×844 the document width stayed at 390px. This checks the local presentation only.
- Rendered the 5m and 15m coverage maps at 390×844: the strips resolved to 20 and 60 columns respectively, with a 298px content width and no document overflow. Snapshot values were mocked, not from the live archive.
- Verified the coverage strip’s screen-reader label against mocked UP-only and DOWN-only slots; it reported exact counts for both-sided, one-sided, and empty slots, along with the first-tracked-to-close interval.
- The root Markets detail summary now reads `LATEST N OF M` before its incomplete/untracked counts. `M` is the number of sampled markets for that asset/horizon; `N` is the recent detail list (capped at 12), while the coverage ratios use the broader sample. This keeps the compact status honest about its scope.
- Rendered the root Markets archive-coverage panel and root Predictions tape/pair details in Playwright with mocked market responses. At 390×844 and 320×720 phone sizes, the document had no horizontal overflow; prediction controls retained 40px touch targets, the fee grid resolved to two columns, and the tape used the full-height mobile sheet. The phone layout hides the repeated hero summary so the first live prediction card begins within the initial viewport. This was a local static preview, not an authenticated or deployed cockpit check. Other root API calls naturally returned errors from the static-file preview, so this does not validate the integrated server or production data.
- Rechecked the exact public apex `https://polymarketaipredictions.com/` in Playwright on 2026-10-25. It redirects to `https://www.polymarketaipredictions.com/` and serves the marketing page, whose “Fleet cockpit” link targets the cockpit host root. That cockpit root returned HTTP 401 in this unauthenticated browser session. In source, the unversioned `/` handler in `scripts/cockpit.py` serves `scripts/cockpit_ui.html`; `/v3`, `/v4`, and `/v5` are separate routes. No deployment was attempted, so source changes are not claimed to be live at the public cockpit.
- **Root Predictions phone focus (2026-10-26 09:50 UTC):** made the active root cockpit tab available as a body data attribute and used it to optimize only the unversioned-root Predictions view on phones. The global bot-filter controls stay available in a compact 44px row; the terminal line and live-fill strip are shortened, while fleet cash flow and live fills stay visible. This moves the quick asset/window controls and first forecast placeholder higher on screen without altering navigation, alarm behavior, or any strategy path. Playwright CLI static preview snapshots at 390×844 and 320×780 showed the expected active-tab state, preserved bot-filter visibility in the 44px row, 28px terminal strip, 30px live-fill strip, and a 320px document width; a 1280×900 check showed the global bot row visible and no horizontal overflow. The static preview has no authenticated API, so the unavailable-feed placeholders are expected and no live data or production deployment is claimed.
- **Oracle3 source and fee-rule follow-up (2026-10-26 09:50 UTC):** the listed [`oracle3`](https://github.com/YichengYang-Ethan/oracle3) URL redirects to its renamed [`oracle3-prediction-market-agent`](https://github.com/YichengYang-Ethan/oracle3-prediction-market-agent) repository. Both URLs resolve to the same audited HEAD `267fb7c48dae7c4ff8922c02a3a223443e51bf0b`. Read the current README, `oracle3/fees.py`, and `oracle3/arbitrage.py`: the fee parser supports fee-free markets and exponent-1 schedules, calculates taker fees per leg, returns zero maker fee, and uses `ROUND_HALF_UP` to five decimals with a one-increment minimum; the relation scanner reports basket net edge but relies on named relationships and does not establish resolution compatibility, depth, or fills. Polymarket's current [fee rules](https://help.polymarket.com/en/articles/13364478-trading-fees) confirm the price-symmetric formula, maker fee of zero, five-decimal precision, and $0.00001 minimum, but do not specify the rounding mode. PolySparQ++ therefore keeps its already-tested conservative `ROUND_CEILING` estimate and excludes rebates. The root complete-set disclosure now links the official rule and labels the local round-up as conservative. No Oracle3 code or dependency was copied; no reason was found to add a second relationship scanner for this project's short-window BTC/ETH/SOL/XRP markets.
- No third-party code, SDK, API key, paid query, external signal, or credential was added. These integrations do not alter bot strategy gates or enable LIVE trading. The broader cockpit, mobile, API, and research changes remain in the existing dirty worktree. The focused collector snapshot repair is committed as `1662f70337103b3c962dc572046586eeb6e1b7f5`; the repository’s existing post-commit hook pushed it to `origin/main`. No workflow is present under `.github/workflows`, and no service or bot profile was changed.

The remaining listed tools were assessed individually in the numbered inventory. Their documented assessments explain why no integration was justified: unavailable or uninspectable interfaces, no reproducible after-fee evidence, paid/authenticated dependencies without a demonstrated gap, or added custody, execution, or operational surface without a clear benefit. Revisit those decisions if project requirements or independently verifiable data contracts change.

Useful primary/project sources: [upstream list](https://github.com/aarora4/Awesome-Prediction-Market-Tools), [TREMOR source](https://github.com/sculptdotfun/tremor), [oracle3 source](https://github.com/YichengYang-Ethan/oracle3-prediction-market-agent), [Marketlens order-book docs](https://marketlens.trade/docs/orderbook), [Marketlens access/free-tier docs](https://marketlens.trade/docs), [Marketlens archive examples](https://marketlens.trade/data/events), [Probalytics order-book docs](https://www.probalytics.io/orderbook), [Probalytics pricing](https://www.probalytics.io/pricing), [Polymarket fee docs](https://docs.polymarket.com/trading/fees), [Polymarket Help Center fee rules](https://help.polymarket.com/en/articles/13364478-trading-fees), [Polymarket market fee-schedule fields](https://docs.polymarket.com/market-data/market-details), [Adanos API docs](https://api.adanos.org/docs/), [Adjacent API docs](https://docs.adjacent.markets/), [Dome acquisition and support notice](https://domeapi.io/blog/dome-joins-polymarket), [PolyRouter docs](https://docs.polyrouter.io/api-reference/introduction), [Predictefy API docs](https://docs.predictefy.com/api/), [PMXT source](https://github.com/pmxt-dev/pmxt), [Polymarket API docs](https://docs.polymarket.com/), [Polymarket real-time data docs](https://docs.polymarket.com/market-data/realtime-data). Vendor coverage/pricing statements above are vendor-published and were not independently verified.

It loads a wallet private key from environment and submits Polygon/CLOB trades; the repo says it is unaudited. Do not import or connect its execution path.

33. **[Simmer](https://simmer.markets)** — **Listed:** Connect your AI agent to prediction markets. Its README says replay lacks order-book data and its tape coverage ends around 2026-05-05; live trading adds an external service and wallet-signing dependency. No execution integration.
