Backtest strategies, review risk, and automate supported Kalshi and Polymarket workflows with TurbineFi. **Checked:** HTTP 200, page “Prediction Market Trading Bot Builder \| TurbineFi”. **Assessment:** Hosted plain-language strategy builder/backtester. Backtest assumptions and order-book data must be independently audited; no remote strategy execution integration.

35. **[oracle3](https://github.com/YichengYang-Ethan/oracle3)** — **Listed:** Oracle3: open-source trading engine and MCP server for prediction markets. Finds fee-adjusted no-arbitrage violations across related event contracts and trades them live… **Checked:** The listed URL redirects to the renamed [`oracle3-prediction-market-agent`](https://github.com/YichengYang-Ethan/oracle3-prediction-market-agent); source HEAD `267fb7c48dae7c4ff8922c02a3a223443e51bf0b` matches the previously audited snapshot. Read the current README plus `oracle3/fees.py` and `oracle3/arbitrage.py`. Fee schedules are parsed per market; the implementation covers fee-free and exponent-1 schedules, maker fee zero, and taker fee rounding to five decimals with a minimum $0.00001. **Assessment:** Apache-2.0 relation scanner reports per-leg fees and basket net edge for named relationships, but does not prove resolution compatibility, depth, or fillability. The implementation rounds `ROUND_HALF_UP`; Polymarket's [current fee rules](https://help.polymarket.com/en/articles/13364478-trading-fees) specify precision and minimum fee but not rounding mode. Keep PolySparQ++'s locally implemented conservative round-up and rebate exclusion; no further runtime integration or copied code is justified for the short-window crypto scope.

### 🧩 APIs

36. **[Adanos Market Sentiment API](https://api.adanos.org/docs/)** — **Listed:** REST API for stock and crypto sentiment from Reddit, X/Twitter, financial news and Polymarket. Buzz score, trend and sentiment per ticker. Free API key. **Checked:** Playwright 200; first-party docs and OpenAPI page show v1.54.1, rechecked 2026-10-26. **Assessment:** The current platform inventory separates Reddit Crypto (`/reddit/crypto/v1`) from Polymarket Stocks (`/polymarket/stocks/v1`); it exposes no Polymarket crypto sentiment platform. Reddit crypto `trend` compares activity over the current 3 UTC days with the prior 3 days, while `buzz_score` is normalized against a trailing 90-day activity baseline; neither is a 5m/15m price signal. Aggregate sentiment routes need an API key; free keys allow 250 requests/month, 100/minute, and 30 days of history. No key or data gap justifies a dependency. No integration; keep it out of forecast and order gates.

37. **[Adjacent News](https://adj.news/?utm_source=polymark.et)** — **Listed:** Prediction-market indices, reference rates, and news. **Checked:** HTTP 200, page “Adjacent \| Prediction-market indices”; first-party API docs describe unified Polymarket/Kalshi data, delayed anonymous access, and real-time access requiring a bearer key with read scope. **Assessment:** Its [current API docs](https://docs.adjacent.markets/) say anonymous public data is delayed by 15 minutes; that is too slow for our 5m/15m windows. Real-time reads require a scoped bearer key and external dependency. No current need justifies it.

38. **[ClickHouse](https://crypto.clickhouse.com/?utm_source=polymark.et)** — **Listed:** Crypto analytics powered by ClickHouse & GoldSky **Checked:** HTTP 200, page “CryptoHouse”. **Assessment:** Database/analytics infrastructure, not a prediction-market feed by itself. We already run PostgreSQL; adopting a second warehouse without a demonstrated query bottleneck would add ETL/ops cost.

39. **[Dome](https://domeapi.io?utm_source=polymark.et)** — **Listed:** Access real-time and historical prediction market data from Polymarket, Kalshi, and more. Free, reliable APIs with full orderbooks for developers and traders. **Checked:** HTTP 200, page “Dome - APIs for prediction markets”; its acquisition notice says Polymarket acquired Dome, subscriptions were canceled, and support/SLA after March 31, 2026 was not guaranteed. **Assessment:** The service is reachable, so it should not be called dead, but its published continuity/support terms make it a poor production dependency. Keep using direct Polymarket APIs and local capture unless a specific archive gap warrants a bounded evaluation.

40. **[Marketlens](https://marketlens.trade/)** — **Listed:** Polymarket historical data API: tick-level order book history, trades, candles, and outcomes since March 2026, with a Python SDK and Parquet exports. **Checked:** Landing page, getting-started, order-book docs, pricing, public sample repository, and the [Python SDK](https://github.com/marketlenstrade/marketlens-python) rechecked 2026-10-26. Current SDK page shows v1.8.12, commit `380de6e458117eb8bacb011ef4d5f23991833c16`, and MIT license. The public [sample-data repository](https://github.com/marketlenstrade/polymarket-historical-data) states CC BY 4.0 with attribution to Marketlens; it contains 17.3M rows, including one BTC 5m/15m day with 12,641,833 rows. Its README describes a compact trade-aligned book representation: snapshots and trades are exact, while intermediate book changes can be folded together. Current pricing lists a free tier with two million rows once and markets open in the last seven days, Pro at $39/month with 5B monthly rows and the archive since March 2026, and Scale at $199/month with 50B rows; these are vendor statements. **Assessment:** Useful research reference, not a runtime provider. The SDK's tick engine exposes latency, queue position, partial-fill, fee, and settlement-delay assumptions; queue modeling is opt-in, and its separate bar engine is a stylized signal model that the README says cannot validate microstructure edges. The sample is useful only for bounded offline comparison; its compact representation is not a full event-by-event queue replay. No key, paid data, dependency, or dataset was added. The read-only concepts of top-level size imbalance and size-weighted microprice were applied to the root Predictions book disclosure using already available bid/ask and best-level sizes; these remain descriptive one-level measures and do not affect forecasts or orders.

41. **[PolyRouter](https://polyrouter.io?utm_source=polymark.et)** — **Listed:** Unified API for prediction-market data across venues. **Checked:** Reopened the landing page and [first-party API docs](https://docs.polyrouter.io/) on 2026-10-26. Docs list markets, events, series, trades, price history, and order books; they say all API endpoints require an `X-API-Key`, the open beta is free, and the limit is 100 requests/minute. The market schema includes `last_synced_at` and response metadata for cache hit, request time, and data freshness; these are provider-reported. The landing page advertises a no-key interactive tester, but one public Polymarket/open-market request through its same-origin tester returned HTTP 500 with `{"error":"Internal server error"}`. This tests the public tester path only, not the authenticated API. **Assessment:** Cross-venue normalization could help a multi-venue product, but this engine is Polymarket-focused and already uses direct Gamma/CLOB reads plus local capture. The key requirement, provider-operated test failure, and unverified coverage/freshness add cost and a dependency without closing a demonstrated data gap. No API key or integration.

42. **[PMXT](https://github.com/qoery-com/pmxt)** — **Listed:** Unified SDK and hosted API for prediction-market data/trading. **Checked:** The listed URL redirects to [`pmxt-dev/pmxt`](https://github.com/pmxt-dev/pmxt); rechecked on 2026-10-26. `main` remains at the previously audited `4a367d812541154002eedda36b0916a3cf68e0f2` (latest tagged release shown as v2.54.0; repository declares MIT). Its current README says read clients require a PMXT API key and wallet address, while trading additionally requires a private-key signer; hosted writes currently cover Polymarket, Opinion, and Limitless, and the hosted service describes custody, signing infrastructure, and settlement as part of its role. A self-host option uses venue-native credentials. The first-party feature matrix lists order-book, position, trade-history, and create-order endpoints; several reported test passes use `MockExchange` and HTTP-route fixtures, so they do not establish live-venue completeness or execution quality. **Assessment:** It could normalize access across venues, but this PolySparQ++ engine is Polymarket-focused and already uses direct CLOB/Gamma routes and local capture. Adding the hosted key/trading trust boundary or maintaining another venue adapter would not close a demonstrated gap. No SDK, hosted API key, wallet, private key, or code was added.

43. **[Predictefy](https://docs.predictefy.com/api/)** — **Listed:** Unified prediction-market data and non-custodial execution APIs across live venue lanes. **Checked:** Reopened the first-party [API reference](https://docs.predictefy.com/api/) in Playwright on 2026-10-26. Its overview describes one normalized data schema for 16 public venues plus a router; market records require `asOf`, provenance, and capabilities, while event and order-book records expose provenance fields as optional and order books can include `sourceMetadata`. Catalog, order-book, history, and intelligence routes are capability-qualified. The docs require a bearer API key and documented read scopes; anonymous requests return 401, insufficient credits return 402 against endpoint weight, and every `/v1/exec` operation requires `trade` scope on an isolated execution origin. **Assessment:** The explicit timestamp/provenance/capability contract is a useful design reference, but the root Predictions cards already expose local source ages, model basis, quote freshness, and the limits of recorded timestamps. No concrete Polymarket data gap justifies duplicating those diagnostics behind a metered vendor API or adding another execution trust boundary. No key, API call, paid access, or dependency was added.

44. **[pykalshi](https://github.com/ArshKA/kalshi-client)** — **Listed:** Unofficial Python client for Kalshi's prediction markets API - arshka/pykalshi **Checked:** Reopened the [current repository](https://github.com/arshka/pykalshi) in Playwright and reviewed source at `main` commit `e42d9f3c491c5037cfe51c7f69ad35074d097255` on 2026-10-26; `pyproject.toml` declares v2.0.0 and MIT, while GitHub lists no tagged releases. The README presents typed synchronous/asynchronous clients, retry handling, WebSocket streams, and a local book manager. Source inspection confirmed HTTP retries for 429/5xx with numeric `Retry-After` or exponential delays capped at 30 seconds, and WebSocket reconnect backoff from 0.5 to 30 seconds. Its `OrderbookManager` applies snapshot/delta messages to YES/NO price maps and computes depth, imbalance, and size walks; the inspected manager does not show sequence/checksum gap detection. **Assessment:** The endpoints, authentication, and book semantics are Kalshi-specific; do not add the package or credentials to this Polymarket-only engine. The generic bounded cooldown pattern was adapted independently to the root cockpit's public Gamma reads; no third-party code was copied.

The anonymous tier is source-IP rate limited and explicitly revocable; production use is directed to an issued API key. **Assessment:** The provider separation, explicit price scale, and cache semantics are useful API-design references, but the catalog and last-trade marks overlap Gamma/local capture and are not an execution-grade order book. The local cockpit already distinguishes quote/source age and executable book data. No integration or dependency; no API key, account, wallet, or trading route used.

61. **[PolyCopy](https://polycopy.app)** — **Listed:** Copy-trading platform with a trader “Copy Score.” **Checked:** Reopened its landing page and first-party [Copy Score performance report](https://polycopy.app/copy-score-performance) on 2026-10-26. The product advertises 0.5% fees on all trades and describes a cost-aware score using finished trades; exact scoring formula/thresholds are withheld. Its performance report claims a point-in-time test of 1,796 traders with at least 50 settled positions and $2,000 cost basis in both windows, reports past/future rank correlation +0.26 and score/future-return correlation +0.21, and explicitly discloses that its own auto-copy book was not made profitable by ranking on the score; avoiding hard-to-copy traders helped narrowly, while the top score band still lost dollar-weighted. It also describes market-clustered uncertainty, measured slippage from its own fills, benchmarks against market prices/random picks, and retracting an earlier result after randomization tests. These are vendor-reported figures, not independently audited here. **Assessment:** The strongest transferable idea is methodological: measure actual copied/executed results after fees and slippage; use point-in-time samples, minimum evidence, and market-clustered uncertainty; never substitute leader win rate for executable strategy evidence. PolySparQ++ already separates Predictions from Trade History and exposes source/book freshness, but no vendor score or copy-trading API is needed. No account, fee payment, wallet, or integration.

The sampled posts did not expose Polymarket condition IDs, executable quotes, a repeatable signal definition, or settled P&L. **Assessment:** This is an informal cross-market commentary channel, not a structured Polymarket execution feed. Its crypto context is not enough to verify a predictive edge for the cockpit’s specific windows. No account join, bot conversation, API, or integration.

The public marketing sample for wallet metrics includes `last_updated: 2026-07-20`, so that example does not substantiate current freshness. **Assessment:** Useful coverage, but key handling, missing quotas/retention/commercial terms, the host discrepancy, and stale example data add operational uncertainty. Existing official Polymarket feeds and local capture cover this cockpit's actual needs; defer integration and do not use its scores as bot inputs. The public OpenAPI spec covers market/trade/book history, insider/whale/smart-money feeds, trader analytics, and watchlists; it requires a Bearer token described as equivalent to the user session. The spec says smart-user scores refresh on market resolution and may be stale for active markets. Unusual Whales pricing currently shows API Starter at $99/month (25,000 requests/month), API Basic at $150/month, and API Advanced at $375/month; the plans list prediction-market access. **Assessment:** This is a supported integration surface, but its score feeds are not execution-fresh by contract, its credentials grant session-equivalent access, and a paid key/vendor adds an unnecessary trust boundary for data already gathered locally. No account, subscription, token, authenticated call, or integration.

The landing page advertises trader profiles, transaction analysis, position deltas, and UMA-vote/dispute views; observed browser requests include first-party app endpoints and a separate `bm-store.org` JSON host, but no public developer documentation, stable API schema, license, or freshness contract was found. **Assessment:** Event grouping is a useful browsing pattern and has been adapted to the existing Gamma-backed root Market Explorer search view; the implementation uses Gamma event metadata, not Betmoar data. The undocumented feeds are not a reliable integration contract, and its trader/UMA/news coverage does not add a verified signal for configured short crypto windows. No Betmoar endpoint, account, credential, or code integration.

It states its recommended filters were selected from 4,800 combinations. The REST API requires a bearer key and premium/trial access; the trial requires a card. **Assessment:** The point-in-time and cost-adjustment ideas are useful to scrutinize, but performance remains vendor-reported and the underlying wallet/sample dataset, complete scoring weights, and selection procedure are not reproducible from the inspected pages. The first-party `polyguana.net` resolved-market page provides live CSV/JSONL and a 31-field schema at version `2026-10-09`; the data contract says it is for citation/research, not a trading API. A fresh streamed JSONL scan on 2026-10-26 contained 10,109 rows through `2026-10-26T10:44:00Z`; 215 were BTC/ETH/SOL/XRP 5m/15m Up-or-Down markets (39 BTC 5m, 40 each ETH/SOL/XRP 5m, and 14 each asset 15m). All 215 were `essential` archive tier with zero preserved price-curve points. The 31-field schema has market IDs/questions and settlement data but no CLOB token ID or condition ID. **Assessment:** The archive offers secondary final-outcome context, but short-window coverage is sparse and cannot replay entry-time prices; joining it to local trades by text/time alone would be ambiguous. Keep it out of accounting and all signal/order paths; no dataset import, wallet, vault, points, or cross-venue trading integration.

### 🔹 Arbitrage tools

95. **[ArbBets](https://getarbitragebets.com/?utm_source=polymark.et)** — **Listed:** Prediction market arbitrage finder for Polymarket, Kalshi, and Opinion. Scan 990+ markets, get fee-adjusted ROI, and automate with an API. **Checked:** Reopened its homepage, [API docs](https://getarbitragebets.com/docs), and [pricing](https://getarbitragebets.com/pricing) in Playwright on 2026-10-26. The homepage claims 100+ opportunities/day and shows a cross-venue “Fed Decision” example with a 4.2% spread; the public example does not expose synchronized quote ages, full fee assumptions, or matched resolution rules. The docs advertise a bearer-key REST API and 5,000 monthly credits on the $299/month Premium plan, but endpoint details are labeled “in progress.” Pricing says lower tiers start at $59/month, while the scanner is on Pro at $149/month. **Assessment:** Cross-venue arbitrage requires equivalent settlement criteria, current executable depth, synchronized prices, venue fees, and operationally available balances. The displayed percentage alone cannot establish net executable profit. Our project is Polymarket-only and already calculates fee-aware local visible-book scenarios and shows source rules; no paid API, external bot, or second-venue execution adapter is justified.

96. **[Eventarb](https://www.eventarb.com/?utm_source=polymark.et)** — **Listed:** Find arbitrage between Kalshi, Polymarket, and Robinhood event contracts. See position size, fees, payout, and annualized return. **Checked:** Reopened its [calculator](https://www.eventarb.com/) in Playwright on 2026-10-26. It supports binary and multiple-choice baskets and describes Yes+No, all-Yes, and all-No payoff sets; annualized return is linear, not compounded. Its displayed fee assumptions say Polymarket has no fee on most markets, Kalshi fees are rounded up per order, and Robinhood charges $0.02 per contract. **Assessment:** The explicit payout and fee walkthrough is useful; fee assumptions must still be checked against each market's current schedule, which may charge Polymarket fees. The root Predictions Board already recomputes its Polymarket complete-set cost and fees from current visible asks and market fee metadata. Multi-venue baskets require exact resolution-rule equivalence and synchronized executable depth; no Eventarb code or external API is needed.

97. **[Polytrage](http://t.me/polytrage?utm_source=polymark.et)** — **Listed:** “offical polytrage” with an X/Twitter link. **Checked:** Reopened the Telegram channel preview in Playwright on 2026-10-26: 719 subscribers and public arbitrage posts labeled with market, volume, spread, time left, YES/NO bid/ask/mid, and a Polymarket trade link. The visible posts were dated 2025-12-12, so the public preview is stale relative to this review date. Example posts call a 4¢ YES+NO complement gap “free” without showing fees, fill capacity, or matched after-fee outcome. **Assessment:** A public alert format, not structured current data or a reconciled performance record. No alert feed, trade signal, or order automation is integrated.

No credential or connector was added.

No feed or wallet-ranking signal was integrated.

Dune’s [API docs](https://docs.dune.com/api-reference/overview/introduction) describe query execution and result retrieval with an API key. **Assessment:** A “trading bots” dashboard title does not establish bot identities, strategy attribution, fill prices, fees, or net realized performance. Without inspectable query logic and reconciled results it cannot rank our bots; no signal or Dune dependency was added.

111. **[Artemis](https://app.artemisanalytics.com/sectors?tab=prediction_markets&utm_source=polymark.et)** — **Listed:** AI-powered investment research and dashboards for crypto tokens, equities, stablecoins, prediction markets, and DeFi sectors. Live data, financials, and a screener cover… **Checked:** Reopened in Playwright on 2026-10-26; the app redirects to `artemis.ai`, and the prediction-market sector remains behind a sign-in dialog. Its first-party [prediction-market methodology](https://www.artemis.ai/docs/prediction-markets/overview) documents Polymarket coverage from September 2020, on-chain raw-fill indexing on Polygon, daily volume/open-interest/active-user metrics, fee and UMA-dispute data, and category splits. It cautions that `SPOT_VOLUME` is notional, not price-weighted premium traded, and some venues have incomparable contract units. The [API docs](https://www.artemis.ai/docs/artemis-api/metrics/prediction-markets) show API-key queries; [pricing](https://www.artemis.ai/docs/welcome/pricing) lists API/MCP access only in the Managing Director plan starting at $50,000/year (the free and $100/month Analyst plans do not include it). **Assessment:** Methodology is useful for a future independent daily market-wide cross-check, especially its explicit volume-unit caveats, but it is too delayed and costly for 5m/15m operation and cannot be used without a substantial subscription. No account, API key, or integration was added.

It exposes sections for total open interest, trading volume, active wallets, transaction counts, and category OI/volume, but chart values did not render in the inspected anonymous session and a cookie-preferences dialog was present. **Assessment:** These are useful market-wide context categories, not per-bot execution results; the accessible surface showed no API/feed contract, source methodology, or usable current values. No cookie choice, account, or data integration was made.

It ingests Gamma into Convex and shows 5m/1h/24h movement. The concept is useful; its hand-set volume and movement score thresholds are not calibrated evidence. Adapted as a read-only, in-window tape panel using our existing PostgreSQL books/trades; no score enters execution.

The vendor says its archive stores full bid/ask states on each change and timestamps them at millisecond resolution. The listed Orderbook plan costs $249/month; its three-day trial only covers the last seven days. **Assessment:** Strong retrospective microstructure replay feature, but the trial cannot establish long-history completeness, and the paid tier is not justified until a local data gap and measurable research benefit are established. Vendor completeness/provenance remain unverified; no account, trial, paid query, or runtime dependency.

123. **[Robin](https://robin.markets?utm_source=polymark.et)** — **Listed:** Earn yield on Polymarket positions. **Checked:** Reopened in Playwright on 2026-10-26; the site advertises a 6% minimum APY, an extra 1% matching bonus, and points yield, paid from the provider's wallet on withdrawal. Its [contract docs](https://docs.robin.markets/developers/split-vault) describe a UUPS-upgradeable ERC-4626 Split Vault that receives merged USDC.e and deploys it into paired YES+NO positions on a manager-selected Polymarket holder-rewards market; the docs say V2 Polymarket positions are unsupported. Its [risk guide](https://docs.robin.markets/contracts/risks) acknowledges smart-contract/underlying-protocol risk and that lending utilization can temporarily block withdrawals/redeeming winning tokens; it lists Phage audits and timelocked/multisig governance as mitigations. **Assessment:** This is a yield/custody protocol, not an operational data source. Yield is paid on withdrawal and depends on external contracts, liquidity, and provider solvency; its own docs warn that funds can be unavailable temporarily. It does not improve bot measurement, so no contract, wallet, or staking integration was added.

## Implementation and verification notes

This audit adds the authenticated, read-only `api_v5_market_motion` endpoint and a responsive “Window tape” panel to the actual `/v5` Markets page in `scripts/cockpit_v5.html`. The API accepts at most 16 validated condition IDs, queries existing observations inside each current market window, and leaves missing book history unavailable. The panel plots UP and DOWN midpoint samples in 15-second buckets, leaves visible gaps where buckets are missing, and shows each outcome’s first/latest midpoint, movement, and quote age alongside stored public-trade count/notional across both outcomes. The Prediction Board also displays theoretical UP+DOWN complete-set cost, per-market taker fee estimate, top-ask paired depth, and margin to the $1 payout; it fails closed on missing quotes, unsupported fee schedules, or stale books and is not a bot-entry input. Fee estimates use the [official per-market formula and five-decimal precision](https://docs.polymarket.com/trading/fees) and round upward to 0.00001 USDC as a conservative UI policy; the [Polymarket FAQ](https://docs.polymarket.com/faq) describes complete outcome pairs as collateralized by $1 and correct shares paying $1 at resolution. The root cockpit now additionally walks captured ask levels for 1/5/10/25 pairs; V5 keeps the top-ask baseline. These additions are descriptive only; they do not affect forecasts or bot decisions. No database table, external API, credential, strategy gate, or live-mode behavior was added. Feed Quality reports non-secret capture switches and the snapshot depth setting from active profile files, cached for 10 seconds; the UI warns these file values do not prove what a process has loaded. It also detects older server responses that omit capture status and reports it as unavailable instead of implying there are no active profiles. Feed Quality shows approximate table/index footprints and now samples up to 500 recent, tracked, closed 5m/15m windows to count how many have any local book row, both outcome sides, and total in-window snapshots. This bounded, read-only coverage diagnostic is cached for 60 seconds and explicitly does not claim continuous cadence or full-depth retention. No active bot configuration was changed.

Follow-up hardening filters out unsupported horizons and markets that are not active, and caps the raw ID query string to the maximum size of 16 valid IDs. The full-depth archive now has a `book_snapshot_levels` setting: `0` stores quote/summary fields only, `1..19` stores a shallow ladder, and the default `20` preserves existing behavior. The main bot and standalone collector honor the setting; example profiles that enable book snapshots use five levels. No historical rows are altered, and no strategy gate or trading behavior is connected to this telemetry.

**Validation performed:** capture-status, market-motion, and market-storage unit tests pass (11/11); Python AST parsing, inline JavaScript `node --check`, and `git diff --check` pass. The market-storage API was also called directly against PostgreSQL. The C++ `polypulse`, `polypulse-collector`, and `test-config-data-collection` targets build; the focused config test passes for summary-only, shallow, full-depth, and out-of-range values. The broader `test-maker` executable currently fails at a separate maker-fill assertion before reaching its parser section, so that suite is not claimed as passing. Playwright rendered both outcome lines and all six tape statistics with mocked market observations at 390px; document width stayed 390px and the card fit within a 332px content column. Feed Quality was also rendered with mocked capture and storage rows at 390px. The upstream GitHub page was re-opened in Playwright, and the current README entry set matches the audit. The authenticated HTTP route, full C++ suite, Android build, and running cockpit service were not exercised. No service was restarted. The browser status sample is dated 2026-10-25 and can change.

**Prediction Board pair-estimate follow-up (2026-10-25):** the isolated fee/complete-set tests pass (3/3), including suppression when book age is missing, malformed, negative, or over three seconds; the existing stale-rollover test also passes. The capture-status, market-motion, and market-storage tests remain green (11/11). Python compilation, inline JavaScript `node --check`, and `git diff --check` pass. Playwright rendered mocked fresh and missing-age market responses at a 390px viewport: the fresh quote displayed the cost/fee/edge/depth row, the missing-age quote showed “Unavailable,” document width remained 390px, and the browser reported zero console errors. This was a mocked frontend check, not validation of a live market feed. The full dirty `test_data_accounting.py` file remains 13 passing / 2 errors: two existing assertions call a removed five-argument `_primary_probability` contract and an absent `PredictionService.set_interval`; those unrelated contracts were left untouched. No bot setting, live order path, service, or database state was changed.

**Historical archive candidate follow-up (2026-10-25):** the new coverage API tests pass (3/3); capture-profile tests pass (5/5), market-storage tests (2/2), and market-motion tests (4/4). The exact API query was called read-only against the configured PostgreSQL connection and returned grouped asset/horizon coverage rows; planner-only `EXPLAIN` selected the window-close and `(condition_id, ts)` indexes. No per-account or infrastructure snapshot was added to this report. Playwright rendered mocked zero-coverage and partial-side responses at 390px with no horizontal overflow and zero console errors. Python compilation, inline JavaScript `node --check`, and `git diff --check` pass. Marketlens and Probalytics pages were rechecked directly at their official documentation/pricing URLs; their historical-data and trial/price details above are vendor claims, not an independent completeness audit. No API key/trial was created, no bot profile was edited, and no service was restarted.

**Root cockpit Window Tape microstructure follow-up (2026-10-25):** added PRICE / SPREAD / TOP SIZE TILT views to the unversioned Predictions modal. Both outcome traces use local 15-second averages from snapshots inside the selected active window; missing buckets stay disconnected. Imbalance is included only for snapshots with actual positive best-bid and best-ask sizes, so summary-only or empty books remain unavailable instead of appearing as a neutral zero. The shared market-motion API adds these fields without changing execution, forecasts, the V5 UI, or bot configuration. The 34-test focused cockpit suite passed, including all six market-motion tests; Python compilation, inline JavaScript parsing, and `git diff --check` pass. The exact query prepared and ran against disposable PostgreSQL 18 fixtures: populated books returned spread 4/6 cents and size tilt +20/-20%, while empty level arrays returned null tilt. Mocked Playwright previews confirmed all three chart modes, price/spread/imbalance axes, 40px mode controls, and no horizontal overflow at 390×844 and 320×720. Static serving produced expected 404s for unrelated cockpit APIs, so this is local presentation and SQL-fixture validation, not an authenticated or deployed-service check. No production database was queried, no service was restarted, and no live behavior was changed.

**Root cockpit Window Tape inspection follow-up (2026-10-25):** added an accessible 15-second-bucket slider below the chart, with a selected-time crosshair and paired UP/DOWN readout. Missing observations on either outcome are called out as “not captured”; switching PRICE / SPREAD / TOP SIZE TILT retains the selected time when that bucket exists in the new metric. This works from the same bounded active-window response and adds no data provider. The 34-test focused cockpit suite, Python compilation, inline JavaScript parse, and `git diff --check` pass. Playwright with synthetic local data confirmed selecting a bucket with no UP sample reports UP unavailable while preserving the available DOWN value, the crosshair marks the selected series, and switching to spread keeps the selection. At 390×844 and 320×720 the document stayed within viewport width; the slider and metric buttons are 40px high at 320px. Static preview API 404s are expected and do not validate the authenticated or deployed service.

**Upstream list recheck (2026-10-25):** the GitHub README still points to `e900f5d576c9341b144b9f3cade5f0871be79471`. Playwright found 19 category headings and 178 categorized project links; comparing the 178 current name/URL pairs against this report produced an exact match. The report’s 179 numbered entries include featured Oddpool.

**Root cockpit inferred trade-flow follow-up (2026-10-25):** added a TRADE FLOW chart mode to the unversioned Predictions tape. It uses the recorder’s BBO tick-rule aggressor inference—not an explicit Polymarket taker-side field—to bucket public-print notional into UP-supporting and DOWN-supporting gross amounts and net flow. The view reports classified and unclassified counts; unknown-only buckets remain “not captured” and are omitted from the signed chart. This is descriptive microstructure context, not a prediction or execution signal. The 34-test focused cockpit suite, Python compilation, inline JavaScript parsing, and `git diff --check` pass. The API query executed on disposable PostgreSQL 18 fixtures and returned +$4.90 net from known prints (four classified, two unclassified); a second all-unknown fixture returned null window flow and a zero-classified/two-unclassified bucket. Playwright with mocked data verified positive/negative bars, gross values and counts in the scrubber, unknown-only bucket handling, and the selected-bar outline at 320×720; the page stayed 320px wide with four 40px metric buttons. Static serving returns expected 404s for unrelated APIs, so no authenticated or deployed behavior is claimed. No strategy input, bot configuration, live trading behavior, service, or production database changed.

**Root cockpit reported trade-side cross-check (2026-10-26):** Polymarket's current [real-time market schema](https://docs.polymarket.com/market-data/realtime-data) describes `last_trade_price.payload.side` as `OrderSide` and shows BUY/SELL examples; it does not establish a guaranteed taker-aggressor meaning. The feed parser now captures that field as optional data instead of leaving the old trade update's outcome label uninitialized. Main-engine and standalone-collector persistence resolve the token through tracked UP/DOWN maps and store the official side separately in `pm_trades.raw.reported_side`; the local BBO tick-rule inference remains unchanged for its prior predictor and PAPER-fill uses. The root `/api/market_motion` reports reported-side flow, missing-side coverage, and agreement with local inference; the root Predictions tape labels the comparison and says the reported field is not assumed to be a taker flag. A disposable PostgreSQL 18 cluster exercised the actual aggregate query with four synthetic trades: three reported sides, one missing side, `$15.00` reported notional, `+$3.00` reported UP-supporting net flow, and two of three comparable directions agreeing. The fixture deliberately included a reported/local disagreement; the two flow totals remained distinct. The cluster was stopped and deleted. The feed targets and focused CTest passed 2/2; the root market-motion unittest passed 6/6; Python compilation, inline root JavaScript `node --check`, and scoped `git diff --check` passed. Playwright CLI rendered synthetic flow at 1280px and 320×720; the 320px document stayed 320px wide, all six stats cards fit their 142px cells, and the displayed agreement was 66.7%. The static local page made expected unauthenticated API errors because it was not the cockpit service. A fresh anonymous visit to the user's designated apex at 04:46 UTC rendered the public marketing page after redirecting to `www`; the local work targets only `scripts/cockpit.py`'s unversioned `/` handler and `scripts/cockpit_ui.html`. No authenticated/deployed behavior, production DB, predictor/order gate, bot, order, or LIVE mode was changed.

**Root cockpit optional trade-time follow-up (2026-10-26):** Polymarket's real-time data schema marks `last_trade_price.payload.timestamp` optional. Previously an omitted timestamp parsed as zero; `insert_pm_trade` passed that through `to_timestamp(0)`, putting the print in 1970, while the main engine's time-window flow buffer also received zero. The recorder now stores the positive exchange timestamp when present, otherwise callback receive time; it retains the original exchange and receive milliseconds plus `timestamp_source` (`exchange`, `receive`, or `database`) in the existing raw JSONB. The main flow buffer uses the same exchange-or-receive resolution. PAPER maker fill simulation already used receive time when exchange time was absent and keeps that policy. The root tape shows the number of time-tagged prints, exchange/receipt/database-clock source counts, and legacy untagged rows. Unit coverage checks event-time precedence and missing/negative timestamp fallback. A disposable PostgreSQL 18 cluster exercised the insert statement and actual root aggregate with one exchange-timestamp row, one receive-time fallback, one database-clock fallback, and one legacy row; returned counts were 3 tagged, 1 receipt fallback, 1 database fallback, and 1 unknown. The cluster was stopped and removed. `polypulse`, `polypulse-collector`, and `test-polymarket-trade-side` built; focused CTest passed 2/2; market-motion unittest passed 6/6; Python compilation, inline JavaScript parse, and scoped `git diff --check` passed. Playwright rendered the new time-source card with synthetic data at desktop and 320×720; the 320px page stayed within viewport width and the 142px card showed all source counts without clipping. Static preview API failures were expected because it was not connected to the authenticated cockpit service. The addition only repairs missing-timestamp research chronology and annotates source; no LIVE, bot, prediction, or order behavior changed.

**Root cockpit optional trade-size follow-up (2026-10-26):** the official event schema also makes `last_trade_price.payload.size` optional. Previously `field_as_double` converted missing, malformed, or non-finite size data to `0`, indistinguishable from a parsed zero and invisible in notional coverage. The parser now retains a `size_available` flag and accepts only fully parsed finite nonnegative values; the existing non-null `pm_trades.size` column receives zero for unavailable size, while the existing raw JSONB marks it unavailable. The root tape reports parsed/missing/legacy-unknown size coverage and explicitly says unavailable sizes contribute no notional. Five new `test-polymarket-trade-side` checks cover decimal/zero parsing and empty, malformed, and NaN rejection. A disposable PostgreSQL 18 cluster exercised exchange, receive-time, DB-clock, and legacy timestamp rows together with known/missing/legacy size flags; the actual root aggregate kept their coverage counts distinct. The cluster was stopped and deleted. The engine, collector, and parser-test targets build; focused CTest passes 2/2; the root market-motion suite passes 6/6; Python compilation, root inline JavaScript parse, and scoped `git diff --check` pass. Synthetic Playwright previews at 1280px and 320×720 render the added size card without document overflow; all eight flow metrics fit the phone grid, with unavailable size clearly labeled. Static API errors are expected for the standalone local preview. This is tape/data-quality metadata only: it does not manufacture notional, qualify a PAPER fill, or affect a forecast or order.

Dock selection routes through each existing root tab handler; the sheet restores focus on close and traps keyboard focus while open. PolyTron becomes a compact labeled chat control in the phone header, keeping it reachable without covering cards or dock items. Playwright on the local root UI confirmed the five touch targets are 59px wide at 320×720 with no document overflow, search filters to Engine lab, and selecting it closes the sheet, changes the existing tab, and updates the “More” label. Keyboard traversal wrapped within the sheet; Escape closed it and restored focus. Desktop width kept the existing sidebar and hid the mobile dock. This local static preview has expected API errors because it is not the authenticated cockpit service. The unversioned source was edited; no `/v3`–`/v5` route, service, strategy, order, bot, or production data was changed. The public apex currently serves the marketing page and is not the authenticated cockpit URL; deployment was not attempted.

**Root cockpit paired-midpoint parity follow-up (2026-10-25):** added a PAIR PARITY mode to the unversioned Predictions Window Tape. It plots `(UP midpoint + DOWN midpoint − $1) × 100` in cents against a visible zero line, and includes a bucket only when both sides have a valid midpoint at that exact stored 15-second bucket index; gaps remain disconnected. The scrubber reports each paired sum and deviation, and the mode reports its matched-bucket count. The mobile mode controls now use three columns to keep all five metrics touch-accessible. This is midpoint-only context: asks, fees, and execution costs are excluded, so it is not an executable edge or strategy signal. Playwright synthetic data produced exactly two pairs from three samples on each side (98¢/−2¢ and 95¢/−5¢), left the unmatched middle bucket disconnected, and showed the empty message plus disabled scrubber when there were no matching buckets. At 390px and 320px widths all five controls remained 40px high; the document stayed within viewport width, including 320px. Static serving returned expected errors for unrelated APIs; this verifies local presentation and calculations, not the authenticated endpoint or deployment. No forecast, bot, strategy, order, or versioned cockpit was changed.


**Root cockpit stored-tape replay follow-up (2026-10-25):** added a REPLAY / PAUSE control to the unversioned Predictions Window Tape. It advances through the currently selected metric’s existing recorded 15-second buckets every 0.5 seconds, stops at the last point, and can be paused or taken over with the scrubber. Replay stops on close, metric change, missing data, or tape-load error; rapid live-region announcements are suppressed during playback and restored on pause. It adds no endpoint and cannot alter forecasts or orders. Playwright with synthetic data confirmed chronological advancement, pause behavior, automatic stopping if refresh leaves fewer than two usable buckets, one-sample/no-sample disabled states, mode-switch reset, and cleanup on close. At 320px and 390px the control remains a 40px touch target with no document overflow. The static preview’s unrelated API requests return 404; this validates local interaction only, not the authenticated service or deployment. No production database, bot, strategy, or order state changed.


**Root cockpit mobile checkpoint-trace follow-up (2026-10-25):** the root Predictions history now renders a small per-window UP-probability trace inside each mobile history card. **Root cockpit longest book-coverage gap (2026-10-26):** root Markets window details now report the longest consecutive run of 15-second buckets without both UP and DOWN snapshots in a distinct, scan-friendly metric row. The value is derived from the exact bucket-state timeline already shown, so it highlights prolonged incomplete local coverage without new queries or assumptions about continuous sampling. It is a read-only diagnostic; no capture, feed, strategy, or order behavior changed. A Playwright fixture with six slots (both, UP-only, none, DOWN-only, none, both) showed a 60-second run; all-paired slots returned zero and three incomplete slots returned three. The rendered metric and six-slot timeline stayed within the viewport at 360px, and the desktop document had no horizontal overflow at 1280px. This used mocked coverage through a local static preview, not live database rows or the deployed cockpit.

**Root Prediction feed-health alerts (2026-10-26):** when the last snapshot is still fresh but the next request fails, the forecast remains hidden and the message reports a temporary feed failure rather than falsely blaming stale inputs. Stale data and combined stale-plus-request-failure states have separate copy. After the feed has first been healthy, a transition to unavailable shows one toast, repeated retries do not add duplicates, and recovery shows one restoration toast. Mobile toasts are placed above the bottom dock and use the available width. Playwright state fixtures returned the three correct messages and verified the healthy → unavailable → repeated retry → recovered sequence at 360px and 320px; at both widths there was no horizontal overflow and the toast stayed above the dock (296px toast width at 320px). The page was served statically, with health states injected locally; no authenticated feed or production service was queried.

**Root Prediction startup/restart alert guard (2026-10-26):** a successful HTTP response marked `warming_up` no longer counts as a previously healthy forecast feed. Startup warm-up remains quiet; after the feed has delivered a forecast, a transition to stale, transport-failed, or warming-up state alerts once, and the next healthy forecast reports recovery once. This matches the producer contract: `producer_version` advances on refresh attempts (including failures), while snapshot age remains tied to `inputs_at`; unchanged-version polls therefore keep monotonic local age rather than resetting it. A local Playwright state sequence verified quiet initial warm-up, one stale alert despite repeated unavailable calls, one recovery alert, and one restart/warm-up alert after health. `git diff --check` passed. The static page produced expected API 404s and no authenticated feed, service restart, or production deployment was tested.

**Root Prediction calibration uncertainty follow-up (2026-10-26):** the root history API now returns a nominal 95% Wilson interval for each model and paired-CLOB calibration bin. Its existing bin query includes the exact resolved UP count alongside the sample count; the API computes the bounds once, and the root UI renders them as chart whiskers, marker details, and accessible table values. The scorecard note states that the interval assumes independent binomial observations and is not adjusted for serial dependence, individual-market forecast uncertainty, or trading returns. This adds descriptive aggregation only; forecast values, scoring, filters, bot, strategy, and execution paths are unchanged. The Wilson formula follows the NIST/SEMATECH handbook's binomial proportion interval treatment: [NIST confidence intervals for proportions](https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/prc/section2/prc241.htm). The focused history API suite passed 5/5, including the 3/4 (30.1%–95.4%) and 0/1 (0.0%–79.3%) reference cases and invalid-count handling. The query's forecast/bucket aggregation ran against a disposable PostgreSQL 18 fixture: model and CLOB each returned the 60–70% bin with n=4, three UP outcomes, and observed rate 0.75. Playwright using a response-shaped fixture rendered both series, interval tooltips, and table bounds; the document stayed 320px wide at a 320px viewport (268px chart) and 1280px wide at a 1280px viewport (1185.6px chart). Python compilation and `git diff --check` passed. The local static page returned expected 404s for cockpit APIs; no production database, authenticated service, or deployment was tested.

**Root Prediction mobile calibration readability follow-up (2026-10-26):** changed the calibration SVG from a fixed 900-unit layout to coordinates measured from its open history panel, preserving desktop proportions while keeping phone tick labels near 9–11px. The chart redraws from its cached response when the viewport changes, so rotation/resizing does not leave desktop-sized coordinates squeezed into a phone card. Playwright at 320px showed a 268×199 viewBox and 9.6px axis labels with document width 320px; resizing to 1280px restored the 900×320 viewBox and 11px labels with no horizontal overflow. The mock fixture retained both Wilson whiskers and accessible bounds. This is local presentation only; static-preview API errors are expected, and no authenticated history or deployment was checked.

**Root Prediction hidden-scorecard layout guard (2026-10-26):** checked the actual mobile interaction, where the modal opens on WINDOW LOG and CSS hides the scorecard while its history response is rendered. The first responsive pass therefore saw `clientWidth=0` and kept the 900-unit fallback until the tab was changed. `setPredictionHistoryView('analytics')` now redraws from the cached calibration response on the next animation frame after the scorecard becomes visible. Playwright reproduced the sequence at 320px: hidden panel width 0 / viewBox 900, then visible panel width 268 / viewBox 268×199 / 9.6px tick text after switching to SCORECARD. Both model/CLOB whiskers and the expanded 60–70% table row remained visible, and document width stayed 320px. **Root Prediction card diagnostics on mobile (2026-10-26):** an actual card mock showed that combined Binance/Coinbase move, exchange-basis, and model-input explanations were silently ellipsized in their two-column tiles. **Root Prediction model-based directional size walk (2026-10-26):** added a collapsed, read-only “MODEL-BASED SIZE WALK · RESEARCH ONLY” detail for each current and next market. It walks the visible UP/DOWN ask ladders for 1/5/10/25 shares, uses each side’s model probability, rounds the supported taker-fee estimate upward, and reports estimated net cost, model-implied expected payout/value, break-even probability, and insufficient visible depth. Each side’s own book age is checked before using either its top ask or depth ladder; stale or missing quotes report BOOK UNAVAILABLE, and the whole estimate is cleared when the forecast or book snapshot is stale. Current and upcoming cards share the same freshness-gated calculation. The root card now exposes separate UP and DOWN book ages, with distinct fresh, stale, and unknown labels in the size walk, so a fresh outcome cannot mask a stale opposite book. On very narrow screens (≤360px), the UP and DOWN research panels stack vertically and their size scenarios form a two-column grid to give labels room to wrap. Ten focused ask/depth/freshness/economics tests and five history tests passed; Python compilation, inline JavaScript syntax, and `git diff --check` passed. Playwright with synthetic values showed no horizontal overflow at 320px, 390px, or 1280px; the 320px layout stacks the two side panels within the 246px phone-card content area, and at 1280px each side fits its 212px column. The note states the calibrated-probability assumption, lack of an established executable edge, and omitted queue/partial/one-sided fill risk. It does not feed any forecast, bot gate, or order path. The local preview had expected API 404s for unrelated cockpit endpoints; authenticated market data and deployment were not tested.

**Root Prediction paired Brier comparison (2026-10-26):** each checkpoint scorecard now adds CLOB-relative Brier skill, computed as `1 − model Brier / CLOB Brier` over the paired sample already shown on the card. Positive values mean lower mean squared probability error than the CLOB on those same resolved windows; negative values mean higher error. Zero benchmark, missing data, and no paired sample are labeled separately. The formula and its limits are explained in the scorecard; it remains descriptive and does not change forecasting, bot gates, or orders. The browser fixture confirmed +12.5% for model/CLOB Briers 0.21/0.24, −25.0% for 0.25/0.20, parity at equal scores, and explicit unavailable/undefined states. Mobile rendering at 320px stayed within the viewport. Focused history tests, Python compilation, inline JavaScript parsing, and `git diff --check` passed. Preview data was synthetic and the static server's API errors were expected; no authenticated history or deployment was tested.

**Root Prediction paired log-loss comparison (2026-10-26):** added mean binary log loss for both the recorded model probability and CLOB UP midpoint beside each checkpoint's paired direction and Brier comparisons. Both values use exactly the resolved, captured sample with both probabilities; exact 0 and 1 probabilities are clamped to `1e-12` / `1−1e-12` to keep the score finite while still sharply penalizing confident misses. The same paired sample count is shown. Scorecard copy explains that lower is better and log loss penalizes confident errors more strongly; it remains descriptive and cannot gate, forecast, or place trades. Five focused history tests passed. A disposable PostgreSQL 18 fixture ran the actual aggregate query and confirmed paired-sample filtering, finite endpoint handling, and a much larger penalty for confident wrong forecasts. Playwright with synthetic scorecard rows showed the full document at 320px, 390px, and 1280px with no horizontal overflow; the mobile log-loss row fit inside its card and was visually checked at 320px. Python compilation, inline JavaScript syntax, and `git diff --check` passed. The static preview's API 404s were expected; authenticated history, production services/data, and deployment were not tested.

**Root Positions account-snapshot follow-up (2026-10-26):** added an admin-only manual lookup for a user-entered Polymarket proxy wallet, using only the fixed official Data API v2 `/positions` and `/value` GET routes. The cockpit server accepts the address/cursor only in a bounded JSON POST body (not the URL), validates the EVM address, enforces the requested wallet on every returned row, limits each page to 100 positions and each response to 4 MB, sanitizes displayed fields, and caps/encodes opaque cursors. The UI shows the API-marked portfolio value, position rows, per-row entry/mark/P&L, archived/redeemable/mergeable labels, and cursor pagination. It does not persist an address, request credentials, poll automatically, write to Polymarket, or merge account-wide values into per-bot ledger P&L. The account panel and endpoint are admin-only; demos remain read-only. Eight focused backend/UI tests passed, including authorization, response validation, page behavior, safe host and response bound, and rate-limit handling. A disposable generated address (not an operator wallet) was queried against the actual first-party API and returned HTTP-valid empty positions plus a $0 portfolio-value result; no user account was queried. Playwright with synthetic rows verified wallet matching, pagination, hostile title escaping, clear behavior, and no horizontal overflow at 320px, 390px, or 1280px; the 320px screen was visually inspected. Python compilation, inline JavaScript syntax, and `git diff --check` passed. No production account, bot, service, strategy, order, private credential, or deployed cockpit was touched.

**Recheck of formerly unreachable listings (2026-10-26):** revisited twelve previously unresolved higher-relevance destinations individually in Playwright. PolyMaster, PolyTale, PolyOracle, Verso, Polysimplr, `future.fun`, and Converge failed DNS resolution; PolyRadar and Polytrend closed the connection; PredScan failed TLS; Polyguana redirected to an X.com page that returned 403. Astron's current official host also failed DNS even though a search-indexed copy still advertises Raven and a “98%” short-horizon accuracy claim. These current access failures do not prove shutdown; the indexed Astron copy is not a live product/API or reproducible performance record. The individual entries now include the date and observed result. No external result source or trading signal was added.

**Root Execution fill-metric correction (2026-10-26):** the old 24-hour fill rate counted only `status='filled'`, so orders cancelled after partial executions disappeared; maker conversion divided fully filled maker/hybrid rows by all stored maker/hybrid orders, including rejects; and quote lifetime multiplied a terminal-status average by all rows while substituting `now()` for missing terminal times. A local read-only PAPER audit found 61 cancelled order records with positive fill-ledger rows and 0 filled/partial-status orders missing a positive fill row. It also found 2,617 rejected maker orders with no terminal timestamp. At a contemporaneous 24-hour snapshot, the old fill-rate value was 13.29% versus 14.64% using positive fill rows; old maker/hybrid conversion was 5.83% versus 19.64% across 1,548 non-rejected post-only attempts, 304 of which had fill rows. The old quote-lifetime formula yielded about 9.5 hours because rejected rows were treated as lasting until `now()`; the corrected mean across 1,548 closed post-only orders with valid terminal times was about 58.5 seconds. Execution now labels fill-ledger order rate, post-only conversion, and post-only lifetime separately; none is a profitability metric. Playwright with a synthetic 6-order fixture verified 50.0% ledger fill rate, 66.7% post-only conversion, and a 6,000 ms weighted lifetime, including a cancelled order with fills. At 320×780 and 390×844 the document width matched the viewport; the four KPI cards remained in two columns. One focused rejection-history test, Python compilation, inline JavaScript syntax, and `git diff --check` passed. The static preview used fabricated API responses and returned expected 404s for unrelated endpoints. No production cockpit, database row, bot, strategy, order, or trading mode was changed.

The 24-hour incidence caption also says it counts order IDs, not completed positions, and the lifetime metric names its valid-time sample. KPI cards have more space, larger secondary text, numeric alignment, and a tap-sized methodology disclosure; the guide stacks into one column on phone widths. **Collector full-book snapshot repair (2026-10-26):** Polymarket’s official [Real-Time Data documentation](https://docs.polymarket.com/market-data/realtime-data) distinguishes full `book` events carrying bid/ask arrays from `price_change` events carrying only changed levels. The collector previously sampled the first item from either event, discarded both depth arrays, and persisted empty JSON depth—so a delta could masquerade as BBO and `book_snapshot_levels` had no effect in this binary. Added a collector-local `OrderBook` accumulator that seeds from a full book, applies every subsequent delta, and snapshots reconstructed state at the existing 1 Hz/token limit. It filters invalid snapshot levels and negative delta sizes, keeps best-first sides, and persists the configured 0–20 levels through the existing PostgreSQL path; the engine’s separate trading book and execution decisions are untouched. A focused C++ regression test covers delta-before-seed rejection, unsorted/invalid full snapshots, BBO extraction, delta application/removal, and a one-sided book. `cmake -S . -B build -G Ninja -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Release`, the `test-collector-book-snapshot` and `polypulse-collector` targets, and the focused CTest all passed. No live feed, bot service, order, production database, or deployed cockpit was started or changed.

**Snapshot capture-time preservation (2026-10-26):** follow-up tracing showed that the engine and collector hand snapshots to a background PostgreSQL queue, while `insert_book_snapshot` stamped the row with database `now()` only after the queue worker reached it. Under a busy or stalled writer, historical market data could therefore look fresher than it really was in the root cockpit and local replay. The database method now accepts the sample’s epoch milliseconds and writes that timestamp; both snapshot producers capture the timestamp with the sampled book and carry it inside the queued closure. Calls that omit the optional timestamp keep the previous `now()` behavior, so no schema change or migration is needed. This is a data-observation timestamp (local capture/receipt time), not an exchange-origin timestamp. `cmake --build build --target polypulse polypulse-collector --parallel 2` passed; the focused `collector-book-snapshot` CTest passed, and `git diff --check` is clean for the touched files. A fresh Playwright CLI visit to the exact apex `https://polymarketaipredictions.com/` redirected to `https://www.polymarketaipredictions.com/`, where the public marketing page rendered; this does not expose the authenticated cockpit or verify deployed cockpit changes. Work remains scoped to the unversioned root cockpit source, with no `/v3`/`/v4`/`/v5` UI edits in this item. No production database, running process, live feed, order, or deployed cockpit has been modified.

Focused regressions confirm simultaneous requests share one query, repeated requests reuse cached rows, and a request after expiry refreshes the query. Twenty-two feed/queue/Radar/fleet tests passed; Python compilation, root inline JavaScript syntax, and scoped whitespace checks passed. This is a read-only performance optimization with no strategy or order-path effect.

**TREMOR source re-review and root live-pulse integration (2026-10-26 10:06 UTC):** rechecked [TREMOR](https://github.com/sculptdotfun/tremor) at `66dc9110d28cb496f5a67b1270b4eb22f6315c24`, including its README, [`docs/SCORING.md`](https://github.com/sculptdotfun/tremor/blob/66dc9110d28cb496f5a67b1270b4eb22f6315c24/docs/SCORING.md), `convex/scoring.ts`, and `convex/crons.ts`. The repository structure text names `scores.ts`, but the current source is `scoring.ts`; the scoring guide says recomputation runs every minute, while the current cron materializes `scores_lite` every 30 seconds. The implementation chooses the largest absolute market-price move in the lookback, uses a square-root volume multiplier between fixed $1,000 and $10,000 thresholds, and estimates dollar volume by multiplying accumulated `volumeSince` by a fixed $0.50 average price. That is a hand-set monitoring heuristic, not calibrated outcome probability or verified traded-dollar notional, so its score is not copied.

The root Predictions page (`scripts/cockpit_ui.html`, served at `/` by `scripts/cockpit.py`) now has a **LIVE WINDOW PULSE** panel inspired by the radar concept. It shows BTC/ETH/SOL/XRP active 5m or 15m windows, UP CLOB midpoint sparklines, a fresh-sample movement versus the closest observation within ±25% of a selectable 30/60/90-second lookback, quote freshness, and a browser-local visual highlight threshold (OFF, 1¢, 2¢, 3¢, 5¢, or 10¢; 2¢ default). Samples stay in bounded page memory while Predictions is open; only display preferences persist locally. The panel derives the UP token midpoint from the existing UP best bid/ask and uses that side-specific book age; it does not treat the normalized `polymarket_up` probability as a token quote. It adds no polling endpoint, and does not feed the model, orders, or bot settings. Upcoming windows are excluded until they become active. Playwright CLI checked the static local root page at 320×780, 390×844, and 1280×900: no document-width overflow, 44px phone controls, a 2-column phone grid, and a 4-column desktop grid. The local page had no authenticated API backend, so tiles correctly showed unavailable/stale status; this validates layout and fallback presentation, not live quotes or deployment. The root inline JavaScript passed `node --check` and scoped `git diff --check` passed; no test suites were run for this follow-up.

**Root Live Window Pulse stale-state correction (2026-10-26 10:12 UTC):** a synthetic Playwright sample exposed a fail-open display bug: when the shared forecast board became stale, a recent-looking in-memory midpoint still appeared as `UP` with its movement alert badge, despite the status text saying alerts were suspended. The pulse now treats all retained samples as historical whenever that board is stale: it labels the quote `LAST`, suppresses the movement threshold/badge, and shows `FEED STALE`. Repeating the same synthetic +3¢/60s sample retained the alert on a fresh board and removed both `UP` and the alert on a stale board; one fresh active-market book was captured and a stale snapshot was rejected. At 320px and 390px the document width matched the viewport, the pulse used two columns, and the threshold control measured 44px; at 1280px it used four columns with no horizontal overflow. The root inline JavaScript passed `node --check` and scoped `git diff --check` passed. This is read-only UI behavior; no extra endpoint, quote persistence, forecast input, bot, strategy, order, or mode change was added. Validation used the local static root page (`scripts/cockpit_ui.html`, corresponding to the unversioned `/` handler), not authenticated production data; expected API errors came from the intentionally backend-free preview.

**Root Live Window Pulse selectable lookbacks (2026-10-26 10:18 UTC):** extended the TREMOR-inspired descriptive radar with selectable 30-, 60-, and 90-second comparison windows. The UP midpoint delta uses the nearest fresh baseline within ±25% of the selected target; its sparkline includes that tolerance band so the plotted trace covers the samples used in the delta. The threshold menu and accessible chart/status descriptions update with the selected interval. Lookback preference persists locally; quote samples remain bounded to two minutes in page memory while Predictions is open and are not stored. A local Playwright fixture at 47¢/50¢/51¢/53¢ for 90/60/30/current seconds produced +2¢, +3¢, and +6¢ respectively; each range rendered its own comparison text and threshold label, and the 30-second graph included its baseline. Selecting 30 seconds through the UI survived reload. At 320px and 390px there was no document overflow, both selects measured 44px, and the pulse kept two columns; at 1280px it kept four columns with 36px selects. Root inline JavaScript syntax and scoped `git diff --check` passed. The static preview produced expected API 404s and is not authenticated production validation. This is descriptive UI only; no feed endpoint, forecast input, bot, strategy, order, or execution setting changed.

**Root Live Window Pulse quote-source correction (2026-10-26 10:21 UTC):** traced the prediction-card producer and found that `polymarket_up` is a normalized market probability, computed from UP and DOWN midpoints, rather than the UP token's midpoint. The pulse now derives its displayed UP midpoint from the existing UP bid and ask, uses `polymarket_up_book_age_s`, and rejects missing-age or crossed-book samples. A synthetic card deliberately set normalized `polymarket_up` to 99¢ while UP bid/ask were 48¢/52¢; the pulse stored and rendered 50¢ and reported +3¢ against a 47¢ baseline. The same fixture had an unavailable aggregate `book_age_s` and stale DOWN book, yet its fresh UP-side quote remained eligible. Missing UP age and a crossed UP book were rejected, and a stale board still rendered `LAST` without a movement badge. No data route, schema, feed, forecast, trading gate, or execution path changed. Local static-page verification had the expected API 404s and does not prove authenticated production behavior.

**Root Live Window Pulse comparison reference (2026-10-26 10:23 UTC):** each pulse card now names the exact UP midpoint baseline used for its selected 30/60/90-second change and how old that sample is. A missing baseline says `COLLECTING BASELINE`; a stale feed says `COMPARISON PAUSED`; a stale quote says `WAITING FOR FRESH QUOTE`. Synthetic Playwright rendered `VS 47.0¢ · 31.0s ago` beside a +3¢ move, showed the collecting and paused states, and retained stale-alert suppression. At 320px the page stayed 320px wide and the two-column card wrapped the reference without overflow; at 390px the 9px reference fit on one line; at 1280px the four-column layout remained intact with an 8px reference label. This adds no data source or execution behavior. Validation used the static local root UI with expected API 404s, not authenticated production data.

**Root Live Window Pulse top-of-book context (2026-10-26 10:30 UTC):** expanded each current-market pulse card with an opt-in `UP BOOK CONTEXT` disclosure. At 320px and 390px the document matched viewport width, cards remained two columns, and the disclosure target was 44px high; at 1280px it remained four columns with no overflow. A second synthetic snapshot re-render kept the BTC disclosure expanded and retained its `BTC:300` key, baseline, and book details. Inline script syntax, scoped diff checks, and the project inventory validator passed. Static-preview API errors were expected and do not validate authenticated production data. A read-only Playwright visit to `https://polymarketaipredictions.com/` redirected to `https://www.polymarketaipredictions.com/` and returned the public marketing page; the repository's unversioned `/` handler in `scripts/cockpit.py` still serves `scripts/cockpit_ui.html`. **Pykalshi source review and Gamma read cooldown (2026-10-26 11:26 UTC):** cloned the listed repository read-only to a temporary directory and confirmed `main` remains at `e42d9f3c491c5037cfe51c7f69ad35074d097255`; inspected its MIT license, `OrderbookManager`, synchronous/asynchronous retry helpers, and WebSocket reconnect loop. The transferable rate-limit behavior was implemented in `scripts/cockpit.py` for public Gamma reads: 429 responses set a shared cooldown across the Market Explorer Gamma cache keys, honoring numeric or HTTP-date `Retry-After` up to 300 seconds; retryable 5xx/network failures use per-key exponential 0.5–30 second cooldowns. No dependency or copied code; no forecast, risk, bot, or order changes. **Root Markets focused live CLOB book stream (2026-10-26 13:41 UTC):** added a read-only WebSocket for the single binary-market detail the operator expands on the unversioned root Markets tab. It sends the documented 10-second client heartbeat, reconnects with capped backoff, and closes or pauses when the detail is hidden, another market replaces it, the Markets tab is inactive, the page is hidden, or the market is removed. Quote ages still follow the exchange event timestamp; data older than 15 seconds is visibly STALE even while the socket reports connected. The stream never reaches forecast, bot, or order logic. Polymarket documents the public market channel’s snapshot/delta messages and heartbeat contract [here](https://docs.polymarket.com/api-reference/wss/market). Playwright with a mocked WebSocket exercised the actual SHOW LIVE BID / ASK action, two-token subscription, both initial book snapshots, bid/ask level additions and a zero-size removal, computed 50.0¢/51.0¢ BBO with 6/7 shares, stale labeling at 20 seconds, and connection close on HIDE. Visual review at 390px and 320px exposed and fixed colliding depth labels: phone cards now give top shares a full row and place imbalance/microprice in separate columns. Both widths retained document width equal to viewport. Forty-four focused Market Explorer/watchlist tests passed with import-only `psycopg2` stubs; Python compilation, root inline-JavaScript parsing, scoped `git diff --check`, and the online 179-entry inventory validator passed. It reuses the existing foreground-only Watchlist WebSocket (up to 12 saved binary markets / 24 outcome tokens) and the same selectable 30/60/90-second receive-time lookback as the midpoint trace. Each market keeps at most 200 recent events for no more than 90 seconds in browser memory; events are token-validated, outcome-indexed, numerically bounded, and deduplicated by transaction hash or event composite. The card labels the result as observed browser prints, and its accessible description says it can be incomplete while disconnected and is not total market volume. Reported venue side is not treated as taker direction. The expanded selected-market tape is unchanged; no database, new API, persisted print history, signal score, predictor, bot, order, account, credential, or LIVE behavior was added. Fifty focused Market Explorer/watchlist tests passed with import-only in-memory `psycopg2` stubs; `py_compile`, inline root JavaScript syntax, scoped `git diff --check`, and the online inventory validator (179/179 current upstream projects) passed. Local Playwright injected synthetic events and confirmed duplicate-hash dedupe, separate UP/DOWN attribution, zero-price gross accounting, invalid-price and wrong-token rejection, and the 30/60-second lookback selector; at 320px the UP and DOWN print summaries appeared in separate labeled rows with 228px text areas and the document stayed 320px; at 390px the rows measured 298px and the document stayed 390px. This was synthetic local rendering only: the page's unauthenticated cockpit API calls failed as expected, so this does not verify an authenticated deployment, a real exchange stream, or total venue volume.

Investigated the deployed-cap rejection reported as `$0.03 + $25.00 > $23.58`: an equity-deployed-percentage clamp was still active while equity sizing was disabled. Set that clamp to zero in the Endgame and Prediction 2M PAPER profiles; Endgame's fresh heartbeat now reports a $500 effective deployed cap, while Prediction 2M's loaded profile and hot-reload logs show the $500 cap but its older heartbeat schema does not expose `risk_limits`. **Root Watchlist observed-print floor control (2026-10-26 23:50 UTC):** carried PolyInsider's notable-print presentation into the root Markets → Watchlist with an adjustable per-event gross notional floor: all sized prints, $100, $500, $1K, or $5K and above. The preference is saved in this browser, and each outcome summary reports qualifying prints out of all observed prints plus qualifying gross notional across the selected 30/60/90-second receive-time window. This operates only on finite-size CLOB events received while the watchlist stream is connected; it cannot identify wallets or positions, infer a trader's intent, or represent complete market volume. It does not touch prediction or order logic and adds no vendor feed. The local static Playwright preview rendered the control on the root Markets Watchlist; the authenticated cockpit route rejected the browser session, and the static preview had no authenticated APIs or live Watchlist feed, so this is source/UI-preview validation rather than a deployed live-feed check. No tests were run.


**Root Watchlist qualifying-print sort (2026-10-27 00:05 UTC):** extended the PolyInsider-inspired local public-print lens with a `LARGEST OBSERVED PRINT` sort option in Markets → Watchlist. It ranks saved markets by the largest individual price-times-shares gross print in the chosen 30/60/90-second browser receive-time window, applying the selected minimum print floor; unobserved or nonqualifying markets sort last. The ordering updates when a qualifying public print arrives and rechecks as the selected sample expires, without rebuilding the cards on each tick. The option is disabled in Search, which has no Watchlist WebSocket sample. It remains descriptive, incomplete browser telemetry with no wallet identity and no effect on forecasts, strategies, or orders. Static preview and source checks only; the authenticated cockpit/data path remains unverified.
